# Tests

Generated by `python -m tools.testspage` from the tests' docstrings and the designs' headings; do not edit it by hand.
Each test file begins with a docstring naming the design sections it verifies (design 0001 §8.5).

**177 of 272 design sections have tests.** A section with subsections is covered through them.

## Test files

### `tests/cli/test_cloud_commands.py`

Design 0016 §9, §10, §11 and §16: the cloud's other commands, each a client of the service printing its words. `clank workspace allow FOLDER --cloud | --local` asks first (the service's words for what changes) unless `--yes`; `clank mcp local NAME [--off]` marks a server local only or not; `clank mcp add` asks whether a new server is local only once a provider is agreed, unless `--local` or `--cloud-ok` says; `clank cost` prints today and this month against the limits, a pause, each session's and each flow's cost; `clank cost limit` sends the limits as amounts, and one that is not an amount is refused, saying what to type.

- allow cloud asks first in the services words
- allow local with yes asks nothing
- mcp local marks a server and off unmarks it
- mcp add asks once a provider is agreed unless told
- cost prints today and this month against the limits and a pause
- cost by session and by flow
- cost limit sends the limits as typed and a refusal says why

### `tests/cli/test_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: every action is also a command, a client of the API: `clank events`, `clank questions` (and answering one), `clank operations`, `clank records`, each as readable text or, with --json, JSON; with no service running, a command says how to start it.

- events prints the newest events oldest first and json lines for scripts
- events follow ends saying so when the service stops
- questions lists what waits and an answer by key records you through the cli
- operations lists the newest operations
- records lists the kinds and shows any record
- a command without the service says how to start it
- arguments reach the service as typed with no home folder or wildcard expanded

### `tests/cli/test_docker_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15 and §3.2: `clank docker status` prints Docker's state, its versions and each running container with its owner, as readable text and, with --json, as JSON; `clank docker start` and `clank docker stop` follow their operation step by step; a stop that needs an answer asks at the terminal (no leaves Docker running), `--yes` answers it as you (cli), and an answer given elsewhere while the prompt waits is reported with who gave it.

- docker status prints the state and each running container with its owner
- docker status of a stopped docker is its state alone with no empty line
- docker start prints each step and the end
- docker stop asks at the terminal and no leaves docker running
- docker stop yes answers the question as you through the cli
- an answer given elsewhere while the prompt waits is reported with who gave it

### `tests/cli/test_endpoint_commands.py`

Design 0016 §4 and §16: `clank endpoint`, the cloud provider's commands, each a client of the service saying what the service says. `add` asks the agreement (the guide's words), or takes `--agree` from a script; `key` reads the key without echo, or one line of standard input with `--stdin` (refused at a terminal, which would echo it), sends it once and prints only its last four, and no option of any command takes a key; a refused key says why and exits 1; `list`, `show` and `models` print the service's views; `preset` writes a cloud preset and tests it; `check` prints each item; `remove` in use names what uses it.

- add asks the agreement in the services words and n adds nothing
- add with agree asks nothing
- key with stdin sends the line and prints only its last four
- key asked without echo never prints it
- no option of any command takes a key
- a refused key says why and exits one
- list show and models print the services views
- preset writes a cloud preset and tests it
- check prints each item
- remove in use names what uses it
- stdin at a terminal is refused since the terminal would echo the key
- a cloud preset says its provider where an engine would be

### `tests/cli/test_flow_commands.py`

Design 0013 §3, §5, §6, §7, §8, §9 and §11: the `clank flow` commands (list, show, check, prepare, run, pause, stop, diff, proposals, accept, reject and next), clients of the API, for scripts and as the Flow view's backup: `list` shows each flow with its workspace, title and boxes, and where flows are kept and Clank's template when there are none; `show` names each box with its type, title, files and what comes after it, the workspace being the current folder's, and the switches of the main slot a run makes (each preset with how long its last whole switch took, else its engine's last start), `check` how many; `check` says a good flow keeps every rule, and prints each problem of a broken one with its file, line and field, ending with a failure; `run`, `pause` and `stop` say what they did and where the run is, each box with its state; `diff` compares two tries of a box, saying how many lines changed, then the diff; `proposals` lists the run's proposals with their states (`--full`: each whole), `accept` (with `--prompt`, an edit first) and `reject` decide them by their ids or all, saying what they did; `prepare` lists each box's first message size against its model's window, saying how each was counted, or shows one box's message whole; `next` shows the flow made after this one, or why there is none, and makes (`--make`) or runs (`--run`) it; `--json` prints what the API answers.

- list shows each flow with its workspace title and boxes
- list says where flows are kept and where the template is when there are none
- show names each box with its type title files and what comes after it
- show and check say the switches a run makes before it runs
- show says how long a presets last whole switch took when one is kept
- check of a good flow says it keeps every rule
- check warns of a file a box sees that nothing gives it and does not fail
- check of a broken file prints each problem with its file line and field and fails
- json prints what the api answers
- a flow named outside a workspaces folder needs its workspace
- run pause and stop say what they did and where the run is
- diff compares two tries of a box saying how many lines changed then the diff
- proposals lists each proposal with its state and full shows each whole
- accept with a prompt edits it first and reject and accept say what they did
- prepare lists each boxs first message size against its window or shows one whole
- next shows the next flow or why there is none and makes or runs it
- flow new opens opencode here and says the flow was built and how to look and run it
- flow new says when the flow was not built and why its session failed
- flow new in vs code opens clanks vs code window and in vs codes terminal unasked
- over ssh flow new opens in this terminal whatever asked and says why
- flow attach runs opencode here on the open planning and says what the one window rule closed
- flow attach runs the command of the services own answer
- flow attach with no planning open says how to open one
- flow attach home names clanks home as a pane of windows terminal gives it

### `tests/cli/test_mcp_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15 and the M8.0 plan, task 2: `clank mcp` adds MCP servers the way other harnesses do (`claude mcp add`, `codex mcp add`): the server's own command after `--`, its options never taken for Clank's; its first word after the name without `--`; a URL with `--transport http` (or alone); `-e` and `-H` given as secrets, never printed; `--scope project` writing the current folder's `.mcp.json`; `add-json` taking an entry as that file holds one. A command that starts with a dash (the owner's own first try, `clank mcp add myproject --myproject-mcp`) is refused, showing the form with `--`. `list`, `get` (secrets by name and last four characters), `remove` and `check` (following its operation) do the rest.

- a server clank starts is added with its command after the double dash
- without the double dash the first word is the command and unknown options go with it
- a command starting with a dash is refused showing the double dash
- a server at a url is added with its headers as secrets
- a projects server goes into the folders mcp json
- add json list get and remove
- check follows its operation and lists the tools

### `tests/cli/test_model_add.py`

Design 0016 §14 and §16: `clank model add` is the console's Add a model in the terminal, in its order and the service's words: the whole pass with its answers on standard input writes the provider's record once agreed, keeps the key (read without echo, never printed), writes the preset from the model picked with the settings as the service fills them, and loads it into the empty main slot, saying its answer and cost; a refused key is said and asked again, and so is a mistyped limit, the preset already written then taken as it is, as a second pass takes it; Local lists the main slot's presets and says what to do when a step cannot be done from here.

- the whole pass on standard input agrees keeps the key writes the preset and loads it
- a refused key is said and asked again
- with a local model running it tests then asks and no keeps it
- local lists the presets and says what to do when a step cannot be done
- a mistyped limit is asked again and the preset already written is taken
- a rerun after a preset was written takes it and goes on

### `tests/cli/test_output.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: the `clank` command writes UTF-8 on every platform, so what it prints reads the same in a terminal, a pipe or a file (on Windows, Python would otherwise write a pipe in the system's code page).

- the command writes utf8 to a pipe

### `tests/cli/test_service_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: `clank status` says whether the service runs and what waits for the user, and how to start it when it does not; `clank stop` stops it; `clank serve --background` says when a service already runs and starts none, and a start in the background that fails says only what its own service said (the owner's report, 2026-10-18: the error quoted another service's log lines).

- status says how to start the service when it does not run
- status shows the running service and stop stops it
- serve in the background says when a service already runs and starts no second one
- a start in the background that fails says only what its own service said

### `tests/cli/test_work_cloud.py`

Design 0016 §9, §14 and §16, decision 0112: `clank work` with no usable model says so and how to add one; with a cloud model in the main slot and the workspace not answered, it asks the workspace's question in the terminal, in the service's words, before OpenCode starts, answered through the question's own route: `n` keeps the workspace on this machine and stops before OpenCode, saying how to allow it; `y` allows it and goes on.

- with no usable model it says so and how to add one
- n keeps the workspace local and stops before opencode
- y allows the workspace and goes on

### `tests/components/agents/test_agent_servers.py`

Design 0004 §10: the MCP servers a workspace's agent gets, and the threads it starts. Your own servers are given to every workspace's agent unless held back from one; a folder's own (its `.mcp.json`) are offered, never trusted: given once allowed for that workspace, and not given again after their entry changes until allowed again. A server reached by address is not given (the container reaches nothing but the model). The agent's configuration carries the servers it gets, each a local command run in the container with its environment (secrets included); the agent runs with as many threads as its container's CPUs. A workspace's server is checked where it runs: in the workspace's container, through `docker exec` without a terminal. Design 0009 §3: the planner gets the tools a server's last check marks read-only. Design 0013 §15: it gets `flow_draft` too, since the planning session that drafts a flow is the planner's.

- your servers are given and the folders only once allowed
- an agent started without a terminal gets its configuration written first
- the planner gets the tools a servers last check marks read only and none before a check
- a folders server changed since it was allowed is not given until allowed again
- the agent starts as many threads as its container has cpus
- a server is checked in the workspaces container through docker exec without a terminal
- clank mcp allow and hold give this folders agent a server or hold it back
- a workspace terminal free command has no terminal

### `tests/components/agents/test_annotations.py`

Design 0008 §5: a figure the owner marked up goes to the workspace's agent, run against a fake OpenCode server: each mark in the figure's own terms (from the geometry Matplotlib's save left in the folder), the owner's words, and the marked-up copy as an image when the agent's model reads images, else what the vision model read in it (design 0008 §7); it is kept in the folder's `.clank/annotations/` (out of git) and recorded. It goes to the session most recently active; nothing is sent while no agent runs, for a figure outside the folder, or with neither a mark nor a word.

- a marked up figure reaches the agent with its marks in words and the copy attached
- an annotation is refused with the reason when it cannot be sent

### `tests/components/agents/test_changes.py`

Design 0007 §5: a change the agent asks for, worked out as the file it would leave, from what OpenCode 1.19 says (`tests/fixtures/opencode/1.19.0/changes.json`, recorded) and from made-up asks; its diff and counts; where it is kept.

- a recorded edit is worked out exactly from its tool call and from its diff alone
- a recorded write of a new file is its content
- an edit that is not exact is worked out from its diff
- a diff that no longer fits the file cannot be worked out
- a windows file keeps its line endings
- the diff clients show is made from the file before and after with its counts
- changes are kept the newest first and the oldest forgotten

### `tests/components/agents/test_context_margin.py`

Design 0012 §4.1 (revised 2026-10-20, decision 0089): OpenCode is told the engine's window less a margin kept free for one step's tool results (`config/opencode.json`'s `context_margin`, 16,384 unless the owner sets another), so it compacts a session once a step's total reaches 229,376 tokens (262,144 less twice 16,384), before that step's results, sent with the next request, can push the request and its answer's room past the engine's window (KVMem refuses such a request: a session failed so at step 143, 35 tokens under the old line of 245,760). The margin is never more than a quarter of the window, and 0 tells OpenCode the whole window. What `prepare` writes, what the folder page is told and the Monitor's compaction line agree, and the Monitor still shows the engine's own window. The sessions are synthetic.

- the margin is 16384 by default in a readable settings file and never negative
- opencode is told the window less the margin and compacts before a steps results can overflow it
- the margin is at most a quarter of the window and 0 tells opencode the whole window
- prepare tells opencode the window less the margin and the monitors line agrees
- a margin the owner sets is what opencode is told at its next start

### `tests/components/agents/test_figures.py`

Design 0008 §4: each mark the owner draws on a figure, in words: the plot it is on (its title, and where it is among several), its range in data coordinates in the axis's own scale (a log one too), the lines inside it with their points and values; an arrow's tip and the line it points at (or the legend it stops just short of); a note's words and place; a mark on a legend or a title says so; without the figure's geometry, its place in the image.

- a box is said in data coordinates with the lines inside it
- a circle holds only what its ellipse holds and a log axis is read in its scale
- an arrow says what its tip points at and a note says its words
- an arrow that stops just short of a legend points at it
- without geometry a mark is placed in the image

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_servers.py`

Design 0013 §4, §5 and §7 (decision 0062): what the flows' runner needs of a workspace's agents. A session at work in the workspace that is no session of a flow's box, nor a subagent of one, is the owner's own, which a switch of the main slot waits for. The flow's own OpenCode server, without a terminal, can be ended (to start it again with a box's limits), and a window never is. A box's MCP servers are connected on the flow's server alone, those it does not name disconnected there, Clank's own always kept (it carries the flow's tools), the window's left as they are, a box naming none (or an empty list) getting every one the workspace's agent gets; a server the workspace's agent does not get is refused, saying why, while one the bridge fails to connect or disconnect is said and the others still change. Run against the fake bridge (`tests/fakes/bridge.py`), with no container and no OpenCode.

- a session at work that is no boxs is the owners and its subagents too
- a server without a terminal is ended and a window never
- a boxs mcp servers are connected on its server alone and clanks own kept
- a box that names its mcp servers as an empty list gets every one the workspaces agent gets
- an mcp server the bridge cannot start or stop is said and the others still change
- an mcp server the workspaces agent does not get is refused saying why

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_tools.py`

Design 0013 §9 and §15: Clank's own MCP server in the container gives a box's model its flow's tools, each a request to the gateway's `/flows/` routes naming the workspace the server serves: `flow_verdict`, a VERIFY's verdict (its result, its findings and `criteria`, each line of the brief and of what done means that the check touched, met or not, with its evidence); `flow_read`, the flow as the service tells it, marked read-only; `flow_propose_box`, a box proposed after a box of the flow, its arguments sent as the model gave them; `flow_submit`, the proposals ready for the owner; and `flow_draft` (design 0013 §15), a whole flow drafted in a planning session, sent as the model gave it. Each answer is what the service said; a refusal is the tool's error, in the service's words; a server that does not know its workspace asks nothing. None writes anything in the folder.

- flow verdict is listed with its result findings and criteria
- flow verdict asks the gateways flows route naming its workspace and answers what the service said
- flow verdict sends the criteria as the model gave them so the services refusal names what to fix
- a refusal is the tools error and a server that does not know its workspace gives none
- the tools of a flow are listed and flow read is marked read only
- flow propose box sends its arguments with its workspace and answers what the service said
- flow submit sends its summary and flow read asks with its workspace
- flow draft sends the whole flow as the model gave it naming its workspace

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_tree.py`

Design 0012 §6.2-§6.4 and §11 step 6 (decision 0050): what the Monitor's Flow panel draws of a session's flow, as a tree. The owner's message is the root and the main branches (a plan's steps, the todo list's items, or the owner's messages) are its children, each marked by its state, a planned one on a line not taken yet; a branch's work comes in time order: a step's calls made at once are one node that fans out to each, a run of one tool folds into one node (`read ×3`), a call that failed then passed folds into its tries; a subagent is a branch of its own, with its own flow's work when it is given; the owner's note joins the branch of the step under way when it was sent; the step working now is the newest node; a step that left the context is drawn apart from one the owner stopped and kept; a dead end ends in its mark; steps undone are a side lane, folded; a compaction is a band, and the branches above it fold into it. Folds: a done branch folds to one line, the branch in progress is always open, a fold that holds the newest node is open while it works, and the owner's own fold or open wins over the default. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): a hold is drawn on its step's line, before its work, "held by you 12:22 to 12:25", and one a note closed says so ("until a note"). Design 0012 §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10): steps undone say that u redoes them until they are final, and then that they are; a result the Guide cleared says "result cleared by you" with the tokens it freed, one OpenCode cleared itself only that it is cleared. The flows are made by step 5's `Flow` from synthetic sessions.

- the owner is the root and the main branches are its children each marked by its state
- a branchs calls come in time order and a run of one tool folds into one node
- calls made at once are one node that fans out to each
- a call that failed then passed folds as its tries
- steps that join no branch hang from a branch of their own
- a subagent is a branch of its own with its own flows work when given
- a note joins the branch of the step under way when it was sent with its state
- the step working now is the newest node
- a step that left the context is drawn apart from one stopped and kept
- a dead end ends in its mark and a failed call still under way does not
- steps undone are a side lane folded
- a result cleared by you and steps undone for good are drawn so
- a compaction is a band and the branches above it fold into it
- a branch keeps its id when a compaction comes so the owners folds and cursor stay on it
- a done branch folds to one line and the branch in progress is always open
- the owners fold or open wins over the default
- a fold that holds the newest node is open by default
- a hold is drawn held by you from when to when
- a hold a note closed is drawn until the note

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.2, §9.3 and §9.6 (§11 step 9): what the Guide offers for the session the Monitor follows, a pure function of its flow, the server that runs it, its context totals and the gateway's figures of its requests.

- each action says its key what it does its exact call and its cost
- an action that cannot run says why
- the reading speed comes from the gateways records else the time is not known
- each actions state is read from the flow

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_actions.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.2, §9.3 and §9.6 (§11 step 9), decisions 0054 and 0079: the Guide's actions, run against two fake OpenCode servers in one workspace's container (`tests/fakes/opencode.py`) with the bridge on this machine.

- stop aborts the session and rejects a waiting ask on the server that runs it
- a note carries the agent the model and what it is about
- a note while a call waits for approval rejects that call with the note
- compact now asks summarize with the agent role and auto on and returns at once
- each action leaves an agents acted event with who did it
- a rules note goes through the guide as the rule
- an action that cannot run sends nothing and says why
- the routes offer the actions act and refuse with the reason

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_undo_actions.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10), decisions 0056 and 0079: the Guide's Undo, Redo and Drop, run against two fake OpenCode servers in one workspace's container (`tests/fakes/opencode.py`) with the bridge on this machine.

- the session is found on its server however slowly its stream begins
- undo reverts at the previous steps last part on the server that runs it
- undo the turn names your message and gives its text back
- redo unreverts and each action leaves an agents acted event
- an undo the session moved on from is refused and sends nothing
- drop sends the whole part with its time compacted and the flow says by you
- a drop of the step in progress sends nothing and says why
- the routes offer undo and drop act and refuse with the reason
- an undo that names no revert is refused and sends nothing
- an undo that would now take back more than it showed is refused
- the fake opencode reverts as opencode does
- an undo opencode takes further back gives back the words it took and says so

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_undo_and_drop.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10), decision 0056: what the Guide offers for Undo (`u`) and Drop (`x`), a pure function of the session's flow, the server that runs it, its context totals and the gateway's figures.

- undo cuts at the previous steps last part and previews its steps and files
- a step with nothing before its last part is taken back with the step after it
- undo of the first step after your message is refused and offers undo the turn
- undo waits for the session to stop and redo follows until your next message
- drop is refused for the step in progress and says what it frees and costs
- undo and drop states are read from the flow

### `tests/components/agents/test_look.py`

Design 0008 §7: the agent's tool `look_at_figure` (Clank's own MCP server in the container) asks the vision model, through the gateway's role `vision`, about a figure of the folder, and gives the figure's geometry beside the answer (its plots, their axes and lines, as Matplotlib's save left them); when the vision model does not answer (vision is off), the answer says why and the geometry is still given; a file that is not a PNG or a JPEG is refused.

- the vision model answers about a figure with its geometry beside
- without the vision model the geometry is still given and a non image is refused

### `tests/components/agents/test_model_changed.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decision 0062): after a flow's box switched the main slot, the owner's window in the workspace is told its model changed: in the event `agents.model_changed` (which VS Code shows), and in a notice the review's view carries for This folder, until OpenCode is next started there. A preset whose context is smaller than what one of the owner's own sessions holds is named as too small for it; a box's session is not the owner's. The window itself is left as it is: its configuration changes only when it next starts. Run against the fake bridge, with no container and no OpenCode.

- the owners window is told in an event and on the reviews view
- with no window open the next opencode started is said to get it
- a preset with a smaller context than the owners session holds is named
- the windows configuration changes only when it next starts which ends the notice

### `tests/components/agents/test_modes.py`

Design 0009 §7-§8: the mode as the owner sees it (plan, review or auto) and handing a plan to the agent.

- the mode shown follows the messages sent in the window and not the phones
- plan review and auto switch the window by what tab does and set the change rule
- with no window the change rule is set and a note says why the agent was not switched
- a plan is handed to a new session in the window with implements instructions
- only a plan of the folder is handed over
- a plan is not handed over without a window nor in plan mode
- the folders plans are listed with their steps done

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_bridge.py`

Design 0012 §3, §5.1, §6.1 and §9 (§11 step 4) and design 0013 §5 and §7: the bridge the Monitor and flows need, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server replaying a synthetic session's events (`tests/fixtures/opencode/1.19.0/session.json`).

- the bridge pages through a sessions messages each part in the watchs shape
- the bridge gives one call whole its input and output cut only past a cap
- a planner session stays planner after a note
- a message finds the owners last message however far back and its variant
- stops reject leaves nothing waiting in the session or its subagents and touches no other
- reject goes on past an ask or a question answered meanwhile
- a compaction not confirmed in time is ended so it cannot start after the error
- a cleared result is sent back whole with its time and the step in progress is refused

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_context.py`

Design 0012 §3, §4, §4.1, §4.4 and §11 step 3, decision 0067: a session's context totals come from the loaded slot's facts only (its engine adapter's context facts, its model's catalog entry), with both engine styles, KVMem and upstream llama.cpp: how much of the window the session's last finished step used (its prompt and its answer, as its engine reports them), how much is left before OpenCode compacts (262,144 and 16,384 give 245,760) and what a compaction keeps (15,000), the share read from the cache where each engine reports it, what sits on the GPU (under KVMem a stored size of at most 36,864 all of it, above it 36,864 and the rest in system memory; under llama.cpp its whole window attended), a figure an engine does not report absent and never a number, the window changed at the next step after a slot switch, and the engine marked as taken by another session's request. A step whose stream the engine broke off is not a finished step, and an engine that stopped, started again or was switched holds no conversation, whoever's request it read last. The sessions here are synthetic.

- 262144 and 16384 give a compaction line of 245760 and a compaction keeps 15000
- used is the last finished steps prompt and answer and left is the line less it
- under kvmem a stored size of at most 36864 is all on the gpu
- under kvmem above 36864 the rest is in system memory
- under llamacpp the whole window is attended
- the cache share is read where each engine says it reports it
- a figure an engine does not report is absent never a number
- a slot switch to another preset changes the window at the next step
- another sessions request marks the engine as taken
- a step whose stream the engine broke off is not the last finished step
- an engine that stopped started again or was switched holds no conversation
- with no step ended the window is the slots and what is used is not known
- the totals read each fake engine styles own fields through the gateway
- a stream the engine broke off after a good step leaves that steps totals through the gateway
- the services context route gives a sessions totals

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_estimate.py`

Design 0012 §4.2, §4.3, §4.6 and §11 step 12 (decisions 0051, 0052 and 0067): the context's parts, estimated in the service from the gateway's census of the last finished step: for a synthetic request with known parts, the parts' tokens add up to the engine's own `prompt_tokens` exactly, each part's share its characters' (weighted by the model's calibration, each part's tokens per character, when it has one); earlier thinking is left out by the template's rule (Qwen's reference template drops it before the last user message) and kept by a template that keeps it, and a model whose template has no thinking has no thinking part; tool results map to their call ids and so to the steps that made them, a result OpenCode cleared is marked, and so are the items inside the tail a compaction keeps; where the room went and what could be freed; and the context totals carry the parts of the last finished step and of each step before. The requests and sessions are synthetic.

- for a synthetic request with known parts the shares add up to prompt tokens
- earlier thinking is left out by the templates rule and kept by another
- a model whose template has no thinking has no thinking part
- a calibrated model weighs each part by its tokens per character
- tool results map to their call ids and their steps and a cleared one is marked
- items in the tail a compaction keeps are marked
- where the room went and what could be freed
- the totals carry the parts of the last finished step and of each step before
- a result the guide cleared says by you and what it freed and is left out of what could be freed
- two answers without calls in a row each get their own step never the one before
- could be freed offers drop only for results and the rest of the loop text to compacting

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_now.py`

Design 0012 §5 and §11 step 7: what a session does now, as the service says it for the Monitor's Now panel. Every row of §5.1's phase table is said from its source (the gateway for its requests, OpenCode for the session), and a request the engine has not answered yet is "reading the prompt (or queued)", never claimed queued alone (§5.1). The thinking and the answer are shown against their budgets, from the slot's facts (§5.2, decision 0067): live counts say "about", and once the answer ended its count is the engine's own and the thinking's is scaled to it, still "about". The prompt is this step's time to its first token and the previous step's size and cache share (§5.3); the speed keeps each step's; the call being written is the gateway's (§5.3); and the loop guard says whether it is on as the running gateway applies it (a change to its settings file waits for its restart, which is said), each part's newest share against its ratio, or what it stopped (§5.4). The sessions here are synthetic.

- each row of the phase table is said from its source
- each phase says its detail and whether it works
- reading the prompt is said or queued and queued is never claimed alone
- live counts say about and the answers end corrects them from the engines count
- the prompt is this steps time and the previous steps size and the speed keeps each steps
- the call being written is the gateways
- the loop guard says off watching each parts share or what it stopped
- the route says what the followed session does now
- the loop guard is said as the running gateway applies it and a change waiting for its restart too

- every action reaches the server that runs the session and no other

- the watch says each thing a step changes once per burst with its last state

Design 0010 §4-§5: Clank's own MCP server in the container gives the agent Notion's tools, each a request to the gateway's `/notion/` routes: reading (the shared databases, a query, a page as Markdown with its properties, a search; marked read-only, so the planner may use them) and proposing (a new entry, a change, a removal, submitting the batch, each naming the workspace the server serves); a refusal comes back as the tool's error, in the service's words; a server that does not know its workspace cannot propose.

### `tests/components/agents/test_one_window.py`

Design 0004 §5: one OpenCode window per workspace. Opening OpenCode in a terminal (VS Code's, or the console's) first closes every OpenCode window open in the workspace's container, so there is only ever one and the mode switches reach it (found live, 2026-10-16: five windows, two of them relaunched by VS Code with a port another already had, so Clank could not reach the one in front of the owner). OpenCode without a terminal (`opencode serve`, the phone's) is left alone; a window that does not end when asked is ended. The workspace's record turns it off; the review view says which it is.

- opening opencode in a terminal first closes the windows open in its workspace but not one without
- a window that does not end when asked is ended
- with one window turned off opening opencode adds a window and closes none

### `tests/components/agents/test_opencode.py`

Design 0004 §4 and design 0009 §3-§5: OpenCode's configuration has one provider, Clank's gateway at the edge, with one model, the role `agent`, its limits the running preset's (so OpenCode compacts a conversation before the engine refuses it); it is OpenCode's only provider; nothing outside the folder is OpenCode's to touch and it has no web tools (there is no route out); and OpenCode runs offline: no updates, no model list, no downloads, no plugins, no sharing, no watching of the folder (slow across Docker's mount of a Windows folder), no reading of another assistant's files.

- its configuration is clanks gateway with the agent role and its limits
- the planner writes markdown only reads freely and gets only the tools marked read only
- the planner and the explore helper it calls never ask for a permission
- a server with no read only tools known gives the planner none
- implement hands a plan to build with its instructions
- the instructions say how to plan measure and carry out a plan
- it runs its terminal interface offline

### `tests/components/agents/test_pausing.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.4, §3 and §11 step 11 (decision 0055): the Guide's pause in the service. It pauses the root of the session it is given (the Guide acts on the root session), through the gateway, each pause and resume an `agents.acted` event with who did it; its state is pausing after this step, then held with its timer, then resumed; its action says its key, what it does, its exact call, its cost and why it cannot run, and the Guide offers it after Compact now, before Undo; a message sent to a paused session closes the request it holds, after the message was sent, and only one held before it was sent, never the request that carries it; a Watch for rule set to pause pauses the session, as the rule, through the Guide; and the routes pause, resume and refuse with the reason. The gateway is a fake here; the sessions are synthetic.

- pausing a subagent pauses its root and leaves an agents acted event
- the pauses state is pausing after this step then held with its timer then resumed
- the pause action says its key what it does its exact call and its cost
- the guide offers the pause after compact now
- a message sent to a paused session closes its held request
- a messages release closes only what was held before the message was sent
- a rule set to pause pauses the session through the gateway
- the routes pause and resume and refuse with the reason

- a header that says kind plan makes a plan
- anything else is not a plan
- a markdown file in the folder may be a plan
- no other file is one

- the watcher says each ask with its tool call each answer and each written file
- the watcher says the agent each message was sent to once
- the watcher says a server that stopped is gone

- a reject tells the agent why an answer in the terminal or from the phone settles the question

- in review the implementers edits to a plan are accepted at once and any other change still waits

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow.py`

Design 0012 §6.1-§6.4 and §11 step 5 (decisions 0049 and 0050): a session's flow, built from the bridge watch's lines and pages of the session's history (the synthetic session of plan s14 and small sessions made here, turned into the bridge's shapes by the bridge's own functions).

- parts become a step with its calls made at once fanned out
- a step that ended in an error is marked not in the context and a stopped one is kept
- a failed call becomes a dead end once the agent moves on
- a message or a part removed leaves the flow
- a line about another session or a status changes only its own facts
- task metadata becomes a subagents branch from its call
- a subagent session continued by a later task call is a branch from each call
- a permission asked for a call waits on that call until it is replied
- a compaction becomes a band across the flow
- the band of a compaction the guide asked for is the owners
- a note sent while busy is delivered at the next step
- two notes sent during one step are both read by the next step
- a note the run ended without reading is marked not read
- steps a revert took back are marked undone
- the console gets only what changed since its version
- a version older than a rebuild gets the whole flow

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_branches.py`

Design 0012 §6.2 and §11 step 5 (decision 0050), design 0009 §4 and §5.1: a session's main branches. When the session carries out a Clank plan (its message is `/implement`'s, naming the plan's file), they are the plan's checklist steps, even beside a todo list, and a step joins the step first not ticked when it began; without such a plan, the todo list's items, matched across revisions by their text, a guessed match flagged, and a step joins the item in progress when it began; without either, the owner's messages and the runs of steps that answer them.

- a clank plans checklist steps become the main branches even beside a todo list
- a step joins the plan step first not ticked when it began
- a plans steps section is read and its other checklists are not
- without a plan todo revisions become items matched by their text a guess flagged
- an unchanged todo keeps its item when a new one alike is listed before it
- a long todo list read from the history is kept whole
- a step joins the todo item in progress when it began
- without either the owners messages and the runs of steps between them are the branches

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_pairing.py`

Design 0012 §6.1 and §11 step 5 (decision 0049): a session's steps are joined to the gateway's requests by session, and pair with them in order, one model call at a time. An answer's call ids, equal to OpenCode's, confirm a pairing and win over time; without them a request pairs in order by time, another request for the same step is a try of it, the last its answer; steps whose requests the gateway no longer keeps, and steps from before Clank watched, stay unpaired; the clocks of the container and of this machine may differ by a second or so. Design 0012 §8: a step's request carries its tokens by kind and whether its engine broke off, which the Watch for rules read. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): and the hold the gateway kept of it, while its session was paused; a request a note released pairs only with an empty step of its own, never in place of the answer of the step before it.

- n requests pair with n steps in order
- call ids confirm a pairing and win over time
- a request again for one step is a try and the last its answer
- steps whose requests the gateway no longer keeps stay unpaired
- a clock a second apart still pairs in order
- a steps request carries its counts by kind and whether its engine broke off
- a step whose request the gateway held carries its hold
- a request a note released pairs with its own empty step and never takes the step befores place

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_undo_and_drop.py`

Design 0012 §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10): what a session's flow says for the Guide's Undo and Drop.

- a step names the part a revert cuts at and the files it changed
- steps undone stay in the flow final once opencode removes them
- a result the guide cleared is marked by you with the tokens it freed

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flows_kept.py`

Design 0012 §6.1 and §11 step 5 (decision 0049): the service keeps a flow for every session its watchers see, in memory. A session joined mid-run is read from OpenCode's history, its earlier steps marked done before Clank watched (run against the fake OpenCode with the bridge on this machine, and read through the API, whole or what changed); a flow is read again after its server's instance ended; the gateway is asked for a session's requests after its steps change, and they are paired with its steps; the plan a session carries out is read from the workspace's folder, and again when the agent edits it; a subagent's flow tells its parent's what it says; only the newest flows of a workspace are kept. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): a session whose request the gateway holds is asked again each round until it goes on, since no line comes while it is held.

- a session joined mid run marks its earlier steps before clank watched
- a flow is read again after its servers instance ended
- a flow whose reading failed is read again later and still marks its earlier steps
- a watcher started again leaves a new flows first reading its own
- a round reads a few flows and stops between them when the service stops
- stopping the service does not wait for a reading under way
- a sessions requests are asked of the gateway after its steps change and paired
- a session whose request the gateway holds is asked again each round until it goes on
- a plans file is read in the workspaces folder and again when it is edited
- a subagents flow tells its parents what it says
- only the newest flows of a workspace are kept
- a session deleted leaves the flows kept

Design 0013 §4 and §5 (decisions 0059 and 0060): a box's mode applies to its own session. The review answers a session's changes, and its subagents', by the mode the flows' runner gave it, while the workspace's own mode answers every other session's; and the service can start an OpenCode server without a terminal, and a session on it, apart, so a flow's boxes each get a session of their own on the flow's server. Run against the fake bridge (`tests/fakes/bridge.py`), with no container and no OpenCode.

- a subagent of that session takes its mode
- another sessions change waits for review
- a session given review waits even while the workspace is in auto
- a session whose mode is taken back takes the workspaces again
- a mode that is not review or auto is refused
- a server and a session on it are started apart each recorded

Design 0005 §2-§3: the session bridge and the agent's outbox, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server.

- the bridge lists each servers root sessions their status and what waits
- the bridge gives a sessions messages briefly
- the bridge sends starts stops and replies and says why it cannot
- the bridge names the windows agents presses tab and hands a plan to a new session
- the bridge knows a terminal interface by its command line
- the bridge gives starts and stops the mcp servers of a running agent
- the service drives a session through the bridge and records who did what
- an mcp server is stopped and started while the agent runs and kept for its next start
- send to phone puts a file of the folder in its outbox with its caption
- the bridge waits for a window that answers late sends to the planner and shows the session again

### `tests/components/agents/test_vscode.py`

Design 0004 §5: Clank's VS Code window is a separate instance of the same program, its settings and extensions under CLANK_HOME/vscode, so the main one is untouched. Opening a folder in it sets it up the first time (its settings: telemetry and VS Code's own AI features off, a title that says Clank, the agent opened beside the files; Clank's extension and the curated ones), installs only what is missing afterward, says an extension that cannot be installed and opens the folder all the same; without VS Code it fails saying how to name it. On Windows VS Code's own program is run, never its `code.cmd`, so no folder's name goes through cmd.exe.

- the instances settings are added once and the owners own are kept
- long lines wrap in every editor and in a changes diff unless the owner chose otherwise
- on windows vs codes own program is run never its cmd file
- opening a folder sets the instance up once then opens the folder
- an extension that cannot be installed is said and the folder opens all the same

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_marks.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): the marks the Watch for rules leave, kept in the service, each taken once; what each rule then does follows its setting in `config/monitor.json`: the mark alone, pause, tell the phone, tell the model and go on, telling the model at most `notes_in_a_row` times in a row for one rule in a session, after which the rule marks, pauses and tells the phone; the count starts again at the owner's own message, and at a step that finished cleanly after the rule's last note (decision 0083). An action that cannot run (the pause, step 11's; the note when no Guide is given) tells the phone instead and says why (decision 0082). A mark on a step, or on a note, from before Clank watched does nothing more. Marks seen leave the count of those not seen; a mark of an ask no longer waiting leaves the phone's list. Design 0012 §3: a subagent's mark never notes the subagent (the Guide acts on the root session), and tells the phone instead; a mark dropped to keep the newest is never made, nor acted on, again; a session whose flow the service no longer keeps is forgotten.

- a rule marks once and each action follows its setting
- a rule turned off marks nothing
- tell the model stops at its cap then marks pauses and tells the phone
- the cap starts again after the owners own message
- a step finished cleanly after a note starts the count again
- an action not built yet tells the phone instead and says why
- a mark on a step from before clank watched does nothing more
- a note not read from before clank watched does nothing more
- a note waiting when clank joined whose run ended after acts
- a mark of an ask no longer waiting leaves the phones list
- a session whose flow is no longer kept is forgotten
- marks seen leave the count of those not seen
- a subagents mark tells the phone and never the subagent
- a mark dropped to keep the newest is never made again

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_rules.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): each Watch for rule is a pure function over a session's flow, and of the facts of the slot its requests reach; it fires on a synthetic flow made for it and stays quiet on a clean one, and each mark it leaves says why, naming what it rests on. A rule over a stretch of calls marks the call where a streak reached its count, and again only after the streak ended; a rule whose fact is not known stays quiet. The flows are made from OpenCode's shapes by the bridge's own functions (plan s15's `tests/support/flowlines.py`).

- the cases cover every rule and the loop guards words are the gateways
- each rule fires on its synthetic flow and says why
- the question tool waiting is a mark of its own and doom loop is left to its rule
- every rule stays quiet on a clean flow
- a streak is marked where it reached its count and again only after it ended
- a rule whose fact is not known stays quiet
- an answer within a tenth of the output limit is marked naming the limit

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_service.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): the Watch for rules run in the service whenever a session's flow changes, so they act while no console is open, and only then (an unchanged flow is not read again); the facts they read are those of the agent role's slot (design 0012 §4: whatever model is loaded); the service answers the marks, how many are not seen and marking them seen, and each flow's answer carries its own marks.

- the rules run when a flow changes while no console is open
- the routes list the marks mark them seen and a flow carries its own
- the facts come from the agent roles slot

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_settings.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): what each Watch for rule does is the owner's to set, in a readable settings file, `config/monitor.json`, a settings kind with its schema: mark it, pause the session, tell the phone, or tell the model and go on, telling the model at most twice in a row. Its defaults are §8's table.

- the rules defaults are the designs table
- a rule changed in its file is read and an unknown action is refused
- a rule given in part keeps its own defaults for the rest
- the schema shows each rules own defaults

### `tests/components/agents/test_work.py`

Design 0004 §1 and §4: making a workspace's agent ready writes the program's configuration into the workspace's running container (the gateway at the edge, the running preset's limits, or modest ones said when no model runs) and answers the command that starts it there; a workspace that is not there, or whose container does not run, is refused saying how to open it. `clank work` starts Docker when it is stopped, opens the folder's workspace, loads the slot's last configuration that worked when no model runs, and runs the agent in this terminal.

- prepare writes the programs configuration and answers its command
- prepare refuses a workspace that is not there or does not run
- clank work starts docker opens the folder and runs the agent here
- with no model running clank loads the slots last good one and says why it cannot

### `tests/components/agents/test_workspace_key_config.py`

Design 0016 §9 and §10 (decisions 0075 and 0111): once the cloud is in play, each workspace's OpenCode sends its workspace's own key to the gateway, never `local`, and so does Clank's MCP server in its container (before, nothing changes: `local`, and no key made); a workspace allowed the cloud gets no MCP server marked local only (held back, saying why) and Clank's server lists no Notion tool there; a workspace kept on this machine gets them all; and the gateway's routes carry the names of the tools kept from the cloud: Clank's Notion tools always, and each server marked local, as OpenCode names them, once the agents component gave them (until then the routes say so, and the gateway sends nothing to the cloud).

- before any cloud provider is agreed opencode sends local and no key is made
- opencode and clanks server send the workspaces own key
- a workspace allowed the cloud gets no server marked local and no notion
- a workspace kept on this machine gets them all
- the gateways routes carry the tools kept from the cloud
- clanks server sends the key and lists no notion tool when told so

### `tests/components/flows/test_accept_command.py`

Design 0013 §4 and §5: a box's *accept when* may also be a command, which the runner runs in the workspace's container, in its folder, after the box's session ended and its deliverables were written: it passes when it exits 0, and the box is done; otherwise the box fails, saying the command and how it ended, and its next try's first message has the end of the command's output. The command and all it said are kept in the try's folder; a command that runs past the box's time is ended in the container and fails the box, and one killed says it ran past its time or out of memory; a box that wrote nothing does not run it. The owner's stop, a cancel from the Operations page and the service's stop end a command still running, in the container, at once (§6: a stopped box's try counts as stopped); a deliverable the command rewrote is kept as the command left it, its copy and fingerprint agreeing. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder; the command's shell, on Linux, against a real `sh` and `timeout`.

- a box whose accept command exits 0 is done the command run in the container after its session
- a box whose accept command exits 1 fails saying the command and its next try has the end of its output
- a command that runs past the boxs time fails the box
- a box that wrote nothing does not run its accept command
- a stop while the accept command runs ends it in the container and its try counts as stopped
- a cancel from the operations page while the accept command runs ends it in the container
- the services stop while the accept command runs ends it in the container at once
- a deliverable the command rewrote is kept as the command left it
- the commands shell ends it and every process it started when asked
- a command killed says it ran past its time and did not end or ran out of memory

### `tests/components/flows/test_box_server.py`

Design 0013 §4 and §5 (decision 0060): the flow's own OpenCode server is started with the box's context and output limits, those of the model the main slot runs for it (Clank's configuration, as written when the server started), and started again only when a box's limits differ (whether its model reads images among them): the old server is ended once no session at work on it is left, and the run log says so; the run keeps the limits the slot has once that wait is over. Each box gets its own MCP servers on the flow's server alone, the window's untouched; a box naming a server the workspace's agent does not get fails at a gate, saying why, while one that fails to connect is said in its log. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder, the main slot's model given as the gateway's routes say it.

- the server is started with the boxs limits and again only when they differ
- a server to start again waits for a session at work on it
- a server started again after a wait keeps the limits the slot has once it is free
- a box whose model reads images where the servers did not gets the server started again
- each box gets its mcp servers on the flows server the windows untouched
- a box naming an mcp server the workspaces agent does not get fails at a gate
- an mcp server that fails to connect is said in the boxs log and the box still starts

### `tests/components/flows/test_building.py`

Design 0013 §9 and §15 (decisions 0097, 0099 and 0101): a planning, the owner's talk with the model that builds a flow. Its first message, kept beside it, names the workspace, the flow, each preset of the main slot with its window, the owner's three answers, the brief, `flow_draft`, the template's model and Clank's template without its plan's gate; one planning is open in a workspace at a time, a newer one setting the older aside only when told (else refused, the open one never overwritten, through the API too); a name an existing flow has is refused. A draft (`flow_draft`) that breaks a rule is refused naming each problem with its field, with the check's warnings; one that keeps them is written as the flow's file with Clank's settings, and the owner is asked "flow built: look, then r to run" with the next flow's answers; a draft again replaces it and asks again, unless the owner changed it since; with no planning open, or once its flow ran, a draft is refused; *Run it now* starts the flow, and a run started otherwise withdraws the question. A draft reaches the service as the agent's, its refusal a 409 in the model's words. The planning's session is started on the workspace's OpenCode window, sent to the planner with the first message and titled after the flow, once that window answers: here, the window whose command the planning gives; in VS Code, the window running, else the one VS Code's extension prepares; a window that never answers fails the planning, said. The owner opens a planning through the API; the agent cannot. A workspace whose container runs an older sandbox image is refused, naming the fix, when a planning opens and when it is attached. A session the window does not show stands, the planning saying to pick it from its list. Each draft is kept with its number, time, state and problems, the latest's values beside the planning in Clank's state; a new planning starts with none, though the one before of its name had drafts. A planning opened to attach takes no window running; attach prepares its window (another closed first, said), the waiting planning starting there, a talking one shown there, none started; one that failed, with no run since, tried again there; one whose session is still starting refused, saying to wait, no other window prepared; with none open, refused saying how to open one.

- a plannings first message names the flow the presets the answers and the template
- a newer planning in the workspace sets the older aside
- a name an existing flow has is refused
- a draft that breaks a rule is refused naming each problem with its field and the warnings
- a brief left from before the planning is refused until it is written with the owner
- a field the draft should not have is named with the fields of what it is in
- a draft that keeps the rules is written with clanks settings and the owner is told
- a draft written again replaces the flow and asks again unless the owner changed it
- a draft with no planning open or once its flow ran is refused
- run it now starts the built flow and a run started otherwise withdraws the question
- the draft route is the agents its refusal a 409 in the models words
- here the planning gives the windows command and its session starts on that window once it answers
- a session the window does not show stands and the planning says to pick it
- in vs code the window running takes the session
- in vs code with no window the planning waits for the one vs code prepares
- a window that never answers fails the planning said
- a planning opened to attach takes no window running and waits for attach
- attach prepares the window closing another first and the waiting planning starts there
- attach to a planning already talking shows its session and starts no other
- attach with no planning open is refused saying how to open one
- the owner opens a planning through the api and the agent cannot
- the flow view follows a flow built and not run yet with its drafts warnings
- a container made from an older image is refused naming the fix
- attach to a container made from an older image is refused naming the fix
- each draft is kept with its number time state and problems the latest beside the planning
- a new planning starts with no draft though the one before of its name had drafts
- attach tries a planning that failed again and its session starts on the new window
- a planning that failed before a run is not tried again
- a draft refused since its flow changed or ran is kept in the history too
- attach while its session is starting says to wait and prepares no other window
- a planning open is never overwritten a new one sets it aside only when told
- the api sets the open planning aside only when asked

### `tests/components/flows/test_built_run.py`

Design 0013 §2, §10 and §15 (decision 0098): a flow built with the model runs on its own until its final review. Its first box, a YOU, is done by itself when the brief written with the model is in the folder, at each run, its event saying so; without the brief, or with the brief as it was before the planning began, it asks, as any YOU; a flow not built asks for its brief as before. A built flow runs from its brief to its last gate asking nothing else: its VERIFY passes on, the tasks its DECOMPOSE proposes are accepted on their own, every one whatever the flow's cap, and run in auto mode, each change they ask to make answered at once while the workspace stays in review, the INTEGRATE, and only then the owner's review. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a built flows run starts past its you box when its brief is in the folder
- a brief left as it was before the planning does not pass and the you box asks
- without its brief a built flows you box asks
- a flow not built asks for its brief as before
- a built flow accepts every task its decompose proposes whatever its cap
- a built flow runs from its brief to its last gate asking nothing else

### `tests/components/flows/test_cloud_box.py`

Design 0016 §7 and §9 (decisions 0062 and 0112): a flow's box may name a cloud preset. Before the runner switches the main slot to it, the workspace is asked whether its agent may use the cloud model, the run saying it waits for the answer: allowed, the slot switches as for any preset; kept on this machine, the box fails at a gate saying why and how to allow it. `clank flow prepare` prices each box's first message at its model's input price. Run with the fake OpenRouter, the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a box on a cloud preset waits for the workspaces answer then switches
- a box on a cloud preset in a workspace kept local fails at a gate saying why
- prepare prices each boxs first message at its models input price

### `tests/components/flows/test_deliverables.py`

Design 0013 §8: a deliverable counts only when written during its try, so a file left from before does not; and a plan's progress (each step's tick, its Log's lines, its header's `status`) is told apart from the rest of the file (the steps' own words, its other sections and its other header lines).

- a file written during a try counts and one left from before does not
- a plans ticks log and status are its progress and the rest its other lines
- a plans fingerprint keeps its progress apart and another file has none

### `tests/components/flows/test_diffs.py`

Design 0013 §8 and §10: each try's deliverables are copied into its folder, and two tries' copies of a file are compared: a unified diff, and the lines changed counted as board D3 says them ("changed since try 1: 7 lines", a replaced block counted by its longer side). The runner compares a box's last two tries that kept the file, or the two asked for; a try that kept the file by its fingerprint only, or wrote no such file, says so; and a gate shows, beside each file it shows, how many of its lines changed since the try before, counted outside the service's event loop and within seconds even for a large file of repeated lines (design 0004's no stutter). Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- two copies are compared the lines changed counted as board d3 says them
- a large file of repeated lines is compared within seconds
- the runner compares a boxs last two tries and a gate shows what changed since the try before
- a gate counts what changed outside the services event loop
- a try that kept the file by its fingerprint only says so

### `tests/components/flows/test_first_message.py`

Design 0013 §2, §3, §5 and §9 (decisions 0060 and 0085): a box's first message is, in order, its type's instructions, the flow's brief, its prompt, the files it sees (a text file whole up to a size, a setting; a larger or binary one by its path and size), what the boxes before it delivered (an INTEGRATE: every deliverable before it, by the box that wrote it; a DECOMPOSE's task: those of the tasks before it), its deliverable and *accept when*, and on a later try what happened to the last one; each file says the box it came from.

- its parts come in order the types instructions first
- a text file is handed whole up to the setting and a larger or binary one by its path and size
- a file holding backticks is fenced with more of them
- a later try is told what happened to the last one
- each type a model runs has its instructions and says how it ends
- integrates first message names every deliverable before it with the box that wrote it
- a decomposes task is named what the tasks before it delivered
- a decomposes first message says to propose each task after its own box

### `tests/components/flows/test_first_prepared.py`

Design 0013 §2 and §5 (decisions 0085 and 0086): every box a model runs is prepared for its fresh session. Its first message hands the flow's brief whether or not its `sees` lists it, and once; then, in order: the type's instructions, where it sits in the flow, the brief, its task, the notes of the boxes before it, the files it sees, what the boxes before it delivered, its deliverable and its own handoff note, and its last try.

- a box whose sees omits the brief is handed it before its task
- a box whose sees lists the brief is handed it once
- a brief larger than the setting is named by its path and size
- a brief not written yet is said so
- the brief comes after the types instructions and before the task and the files
- the map lists the boxes before it with what each delivered marks it and names those after it
- the boxes after a plan say how they use its deliverable
- a box skipped or taken as it was is said so on the map
- with no run the map says what each box before it delivers
- the map comes after the types instructions and holds no other boxs prompt
- a plan a task and an integrate are asked for their handoff note after what they are accepted when
- a verify and a decompose write no note a decomposes summary is its note
- each types instructions say how it hands on what it knows
- a later box is handed the notes before it in order quoted after its task
- a note longer than the setting is cut saying where the whole is
- a box that left no note or has not run yet is said so
- a verify is handed no notes

### `tests/components/flows/test_flow_built.py`

Design 0013 §10, §11 and §15 (decisions 0097 and 0098): a flow built with the model carries its `built` mark (the planning session it was drafted in, when, its brief's file), which reads and is written back unchanged. Clank writes a draft with its own settings over the draft's (the workspace, auto-accept on, no wait after any box, the next flow asked, the mark) and the template's model when the draft names none; a built flow keeps its own rules, each refused with its field: its first box a YOU whose first file is the brief, BRIEF.md, in the folder already and changed since the planning began (an old one is not the brief), each other file it writes there too; one GATE, its last box; no other YOU; no box that waits when done; and a box that changes files runs in auto mode (Clank writes it on a task that names no mode; review is refused), so nothing waits for the owner on the way. A planning's name, when none is given, is the first free of `flow`, `flow-2`, ...

- a flow file with its built mark reads and is written back unchanged
- clanks own settings win over the drafts and the templates model fills in for none
- a built flow that keeps its own rules has no problem
- a built flows brief comes first and is in the folder
- a brief left from before the planning and every file of the you box are checked
- a built flow waits only at its last box a gate
- a built flow has one you its first box
- a built flows tasks run in auto mode and review is refused
- the first free name is flow then flow 2

### `tests/components/flows/test_flow_file.py`

Design 0013 §2, §3, §4 and §11 (decisions 0057 and 0066): a flow is a readable settings file of the kinds `Flow`, `Box` and `Line`; board D1's flow loads and is written back unchanged; a flow that breaks §3's rules is refused, each problem naming the file, the line and the field; a VERIFY has its two lines, pass and fail; a file a box writes, sees or shows lies in the folder; DECOMPOSE's tasks fan out and join, and nothing else does; and Clank's template keeps every rule.

- board d1s flow loads and is written back unchanged
- a line back that is not a verifys fail is refused naming its line and field
- a fail line must lead back to the box its verify checks
- a box with two lines on is refused
- a join is refused outside decomposes tasks
- a verify checking a box that is not before it is refused
- a verify without its fail line or its pass line is refused
- a box no line reaches is refused
- a deliverable that is not markdown in a box in plan mode is refused
- each other rule refuses its flow naming the line and field
- a deliverable outside the folder is refused
- decomposes tasks fan out and join but another box may not
- a decompose fans out to its tasks only
- a flow of thousands of boxes in a chain is read without a crash
- every field of a flow has a label and help
- a flow has its place among the settings
- clanks template reads as a flow that keeps every rule for any workspace
- clanks template runs its boxes on what the main slot runs so a new flow switches nothing

### `tests/components/flows/test_flow_view.py`

Design 0013 §12 and §14 step 6 (with §3, §8, §9 and §10): what the Monitor's Flow view shows of a flow, made in the service from the flow, its latest run, the run's proposals and its events, so the console draws it knowing none of the runner's rules. Board D1's moment says each box's state, its tries and verdicts, its files and when it last ended; the lines say which ran, which runs now and which not yet, the fail line its findings; The brief, checked lists the brief's lines and every *accept when*, the newest verdict on each; Deliverables, Ahead, Models in this flow and Waiting for you are said; proposals waiting join where they would join, marked proposed; a folder's Monitor shows the flow of the active run, else the one run last, else the first; the run log (`h`) is the run's events, oldest first. Runs are written as the runner writes them; nothing runs.

- board d1s moment says each boxs state tries verdicts and files
- the lines say which ran which runs now and which not yet and the fail line its findings
- the brief checked lists its lines and every accept when with the newest verdict on each
- deliverables ahead models and waiting for you are said
- proposals waiting join where they would join marked proposed
- a folders monitor shows the active run else the last run else the first flow
- a flow not run yet shows every box waiting
- the view is pure and its version moves only with what it shows
- the routes give the view and the run log oldest first
- the next flow is said once made else where its brief will come from
- ahead says each switch of the main slot and how long its preset last took to come in
- a brief that is a link out of the folder is not read
- a try that rewrites a file with the same bytes is said to have written it as the runner counts it
- how long each preset took is read once and again only when an operation has finished
- a folders monitor shows the flow running before one queued behind it
- a flow named without its workspace is refused saying how to name it
- a brief in a folder linked out of the workspaces folder is not read

### `tests/components/flows/test_flows.py`

Design 0013 §3, §6, §7, §11 and §14 (decisions 0061, 0063 and 0066): the flows component reads every flow, kept by workspace in Clank's home, and checks it: the file's own rules, then what needs other records (a preset that does not exist is refused naming those that do, when the flow is saved and when its file is read; a flow is for the workspace whose folder keeps it); a file is checked as it is on disk now, and one that is gone is said to be; a good one says the switches of the main slot a run of it makes; and its `/flows/` routes list, show and check flows.

- the flows are listed by workspace with their boxes and a file that does not read with its place
- a flow is shown by its name in its workspace or by its title
- an unknown preset is refused naming those that exist
- a flow is for the workspace whose folder keeps it
- a file is checked as it is on disk now before the service reads it again
- a flow whose file is gone is listed shown and checked saying so until the service reads again
- a flow that does not exist is not found naming where flows are kept

### `tests/components/flows/test_handoff.py`

Design 0013 §2, §5 and §8 (decision 0086): a PLAN, a TASK and an INTEGRATE end by writing a short handoff note in the folder, `.clank/handoff/<flow>/<box>.md`; a note written during its try is copied beside the try as `handoff.md` and kept on the try, one left from before is not; a box whose deliverable is right is done without its note, its end saying it left none; `.clank/handoff/` is kept out of git. Every later box but a VERIFY is handed the notes of the boxes before it, in order: a VERIFY's verdict and a DECOMPOSE's summary stand for theirs. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a note written during its try is copied beside it and kept on the try
- a box whose note is missing or left from before is done and its end says it left none
- the handoff folder is kept out of git once a box that writes a note starts
- the boxes after are handed the notes before them and a verify none
- a decomposes summary is handed on as its note and a task that left none is said

### `tests/components/flows/test_model_per_box.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decisions 0062 and 0063): a box's model is a preset, switched into the main slot before the box starts when the slot does not run it, by the runtime's own switch: a flow alternating two presets switches exactly before the boxes that need it, and a VERIFY runs on the preset of the box it checks with no switch. No switch starts while a request is in flight on the slot, while the gateway does not say what is, while another operation changes the slot, or while the owner's own session in the workspace works: the run says what it waits for; a switch refused as the slot was taken a moment ago is started once it is free. A switch that fails returns the slot to the last configuration that worked and leaves the box failed at a gate. A run stopped, or a box canceled, while its box waits starts no switch, and a wait the service's stop cut short is not said after the restart; a run stopped while the slot switches lets the switch end, and the window is still told. After a switch, the owner's window is told, a preset too small for one of the owner's sessions named, and the window is left as it is. The switches a flow makes say how long each preset's last whole switch took. Run with the fake engine (two presets of a tiny model, real processes of this machine's CPU), the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a flow alternating two presets switches exactly before the boxes that need it
- after a switch the owners window is told naming a preset too small and left as it is
- no switch starts while a request is in flight on the slot and the run says so
- no switch starts while the owners own session in the workspace works
- a failed switch returns the slot and leaves the box failed at a gate
- a run stopped while its box waits starts no switch
- a wait cut short by the service stopping is not said after the restart
- a run stopped while the slot switches lets the switch end and still tells the window
- a box waits for a load that changes the slot and says so then switches
- a switch refused because the slot was taken a moment ago is started once it is free
- a box canceled while it waits starts no switch and its try is stopped
- no switch starts while the gateway does not say what it has in flight
- a flows switches say how long the last whole switch to each preset took

### `tests/components/flows/test_models.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decisions 0062 and 0063): a box's model is a preset: its own, else the flow's; a VERIFY's is the preset of the box it checks unless it names another, so a check and its loop cost no switch; a box that names none runs on what the main slot runs. A run of a flow switches the main slot exactly before each box whose preset is not the one loaded by then, along the order its boxes run in.

- a box runs on its own preset else the flows
- a verify runs on the preset of the box it checks unless it names another
- a box that names no preset runs on what the slot runs
- a flow alternating two presets switches before each box that needs the other

### `tests/components/flows/test_next_flow.py`

Design 0013 §2, §3, §6, §10 and §11 (decisions 0058 and 0091): once a flow's last gate is approved, the next flow is made from Clank's template with its last INTEGRATE's brief (its `NEXT.md`, else the last of two or more files), named `<name>-<n>` and titled by the brief's first heading, linked both ways (`follows`, `next`), and the owner is asked whether to run it now; nothing starts without their answer unless the flow's `next_flow` says so. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- integrates brief is its next md else the last of two or more files else none
- the next flows name counts on from this ones
- its title is the briefs first heading else this flows title next
- the next flow is clanks template with its workspace model words and where it came from
- approving the last gate makes the next flow linked both ways and asks starting nothing
- a run stopped at its last gate or an integrate that wrote no brief makes none
- an integrate that wrote no brief makes none saying why
- run it now starts the next flow whose first run copies its brief in
- later starts nothing and clank flow run starts it then
