- a brief file the owner changed since is kept as they left it
- a flow set to run starts its next flow unasked and one set off makes none

### `tests/components/flows/test_planning_view.py`

Design 0013 §12 and §15 (decision 0101): the Flow view shows a planning while it is open. A folder's view follows its workspace's open planning (waiting, opening, talking, or failed with no run started since) before a flow that ran; its steps say it started, what it waits for (`clank flow attach`), the session's tool calls so far, that it waits for the owner's answer, `BRIEF.md` written with its time, and each draft refused with its problems or accepted; the latest draft is drawn with the view's own layout and marked as a draft even when refused, as far as it reads; with none yet, nothing is drawn; once a draft is accepted, the view is the built flow.

- the folders view follows an open planning before a flow that ran and says what it waits for
- its steps say the calls so far the answer it waits for the brief and each draft
- a draft that breaks a flows rules is drawn as far as it reads
- once a draft is accepted the view is the built flow

### `tests/components/flows/test_prepare.py`

Design 0013 §5, §6 and §7 (decision 0087): `clank flow prepare`'s preview shows each box a model runs with its first message as it would be sent now, from the files as they are now and the active run's state, so it is the message the runner then sends; each with its size in tokens, an estimate labeled as one unless the engine counted it, against the window of the preset the box runs on (for a box naming none, the preset a box before it switches the main slot to, else the main slot's, else not known) and its share of it; one box shown alone; an owner's box has no first message. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- the preview of a box is the message the runner then sends
- each box says its size an estimate labeled as one against its presets window
- a box naming no preset takes the main slots else its window is not known
- a box naming no preset after one that switches the main slot takes that ones window
- one box is shown alone and an owners box has no first message
- the route serves the preview
- a box whose first message is over a quarter of its window is warned of
- a file a box sees or shows that nothing gives it is warned of and no other

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064): a box's model proposes boxes with `flow_propose_box`, which the service gives to the box at work in the workspace its MCP server names, while its try runs. Each proposal is checked when staged and refused with its reason, so the model can fix it: a deliverable outside the folder, or one agents load as settings or instructions, an unknown type, an `after` naming no box, an unknown preset, an empty prompt, and one that would break the flow's rules (in the rule's words). A proposal that passes is kept in its try's batch with an id of its own, waiting for the owner: it is not in the flow. A proposal while no box works, or from another workspace's server, is refused, and reaches no flow. Each proposal is an event with its actor, which says its mode of its own. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decomposes proposal is staged in its trys batch with an id of its own not in the flow
- a proposal outside the folder is refused with its reason
- a deliverable agents load as settings or instructions is refused
- an unknown type an after naming no box an unknown preset or an empty prompt is refused saying why
- a proposal that would break the flows rules is refused in the rules words
- a proposal while no box works is refused
- a proposal from one workspaces server reaches only that workspaces flow
- each proposal is an event with its actor

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_again.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064): a DECOMPOSE that runs again proposes anew. Its new proposals replace the tasks it added that have not started in the run, when the first of them joins the flow; those that ran stay. The proposals of its earlier try still waiting are set aside (replaced) when its next try begins, their question withdrawn, as are those of a box skipped. A run that ends withdraws its batches' questions and leaves their proposals to decide from the command line; accepted then, they join the flow's file for its next run. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose run again replaces only its tasks that have not started
- the proposals of its earlier try still waiting are set aside when it tries again
- a decompose run again from its box while its proposals wait withdraws their question
- skipping a decompose sets its proposals aside and the flow goes on without them
- taking a failed decompose as it is asks about what it proposed
- a run that ends withdraws its batchs question and leaves its proposals to decide

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_auto.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064) and design 0002 §2.2: auto-accept, a flow's setting off by default. With it on, a proposal that passes the checks joins the flow's file at once, marked as accepted by the setting, up to its cap in a run; past the cap, proposals wait for the owner. Auto-accept writes the flow's file through the settings like any change, so an edit the owner makes on the version it has just replaced is refused, with both versions shown. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- with auto accept on a proposal joins the flow at once marked as accepted by the setting
- auto accept stops at its cap and the rest wait for the owner
- an owners edit made on the version auto accept has just replaced is refused with both versions shown

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_decided.py`

Design 0013 §2, §6, §9 and §10 (decisions 0061 and 0064): a DECOMPOSE ends by submitting its proposals (`flow_submit`), which asks the owner one question about them, saying each one's mode of its own (in Auto, its changes are accepted without review); one that ends without submitting fails and waits at a gate. The run waits after the DECOMPOSE until each proposal is decided: the owner accepts, edits then accepts, or rejects each, or all at once, from the command line first, or answers the question from any client (the phone accepts or rejects them all). Accepting all puts T1-T4 in order between the DECOMPOSE and the INTEGRATE in the flow's file, each marked with its run and the DECOMPOSE, and they run one at a time in that order; a rejected proposal never runs; an edited prompt is the one sent. The agent cannot accept. Each decision is an event with its actor. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose that ends without submitting fails and waits at a gate
- submitting asks the owner one question about the proposals
- accept all puts t1 to t4 in order between decompose and integrate and they run one at a time
- a rejected proposal never runs
- an edited prompt is the one sent
- an edit is checked as a proposal is
- the question answered as the phone answers it accepts them all
- the question answered reject all lets the flow go on without them
- the agent cannot accept
- each decision is an event with its actor
- proposals the flow no longer takes wait saying why and are asked about again

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_followed.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §9 (decision 0064): deciding a DECOMPOSE's proposals never hangs the service while a client follows its events, as the console, VS Code and the phone do: each event is handed to the service's event loop and its teller waits for it there, so the driver, which holds the run at the DECOMPOSE on that loop, never waits for what a decision holds. The owner accepting through the API's route, and the phone accepting them all by answering the question about them, both while the run is held, go on at once, and the tasks run next. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- the owner accepting while a client follows the events never hangs the service
- the phone accepting all while a client follows the events never hangs the service

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_join.py`

Design 0013 §3 and §9 (decisions 0057 and 0064): the boxes a model proposes join the flow's file once accepted, each marked with the run and the box that proposed them. TASKs proposed after a DECOMPOSE fan out from it and join at the box after it, one after another in the order proposed, whatever order they are accepted in; one task alone is a chain between them; a box proposed after a box of the chain goes between it and the next, and a VERIFY checks the box it follows, its fail line back to it; a task proposed later goes to the box the tasks join at, past a VERIFY that checks one of them, so the rules refuse it (a DECOMPOSE's tasks each lead to that box) rather than join it at that VERIFY. A DECOMPOSE's tasks taken out (replaced by its new proposals) leave the lines reconnected around them. Whatever breaks a rule of §3 is refused in the rule's words. Pure functions over the flow's values; nothing runs.

- tasks proposed after a decompose fan out from it and join at the box after it in the order proposed
- one task alone is a chain between the decompose and the box after it
- tasks accepted out of order keep the order proposed
- tasks proposed after a task of the decompose join its tasks right after it
- a box proposed after a box of the chain goes between it and the next
- a task proposed later is refused never joined at a verify that checks one of the tasks
- boxes proposed after a verify go on its pass line
- a verify proposed after a box checks it with its fail line back to it
- a decomposes tasks taken out leave the lines reconnected around them
- boxes joined by auto accept are marked so
- a flow that breaks a rule is refused in the rules words
- a box done by you with a model is refused naming the box

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_routes.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decisions 0020 and 0064), end to end through the service's routes and the gateway: a DECOMPOSE's model reads its flow (`GET /flows/read`: the flow, the box it is, each box's state, its proposals), proposes boxes and submits them through the gateway, each refusal in the service's words; its acceptance through the gateway is refused, and so is one the service hears from the client `agent`; the owner's acceptance through the API (`POST /api/v1/flows/<workspace>/<name>/proposals/accept`) joins the tasks to the flow, and `GET .../proposals` lists each batch and what waits. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose proposes through the gateway and only the owner accepts
- the owners routes reject and edit a proposal

### `tests/components/flows/test_restart.py`

Design 0013 §6 (decision 0061): after a restart of the service, a box's operation is interrupted, then reconciled through the bridge: a session still working is followed and one that ended meanwhile is collected, neither starting any session again; one that is gone (its server stopped) fails its box, which waits at a gate. A gate asked before a restart still waits after it, and its answer lets the flow go on. A run stopped while its box was ending has that box's session stopped and its try ended after the restart, so it can run again from a box. The tokens a box wrote before a restart count toward its limit after it. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder; the container and its OpenCode servers go on while the service restarts.

- a restart in the middle of a box follows the session still working and starts none
- a session that ended while the service was down is collected
- a session gone with its server fails its box at a gate
- a gate asked before a restart still waits after it and its answer lets the flow go on
- a run stopped while its box was ending is settled after a restart and runs again from a box
- the tokens a box wrote before a restart count toward its limit after it

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner.py`

Design 0013 §5, §6 and §10 (decisions 0060, 0061 and 0065): the runner runs a flow in the service, one box after another: YOU boxes and gates are questions that wait for the owner; each model box is a `flows.box` operation that starts a new session on the flow's own OpenCode server, sends its first message with the box's agent and model, keeps that message as a file, follows the session to its end and collects its deliverables; a box that ends without its deliverable, or with one left from before, fails and waits at a gate; *send back* puts the owner's note, and that the box was sent back, in the box's next first message; each step is an event with its actor. A session that ended while its workspace's watcher started again is read once the watcher is back, and its box ends. The flow's next run uses the server its last run started. A gate says which try the box it sends back to runs, and when that is its last or past its tries. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a flow of you plan gate and task runs in order one new session per box
- send back puts the note in the boxs next first message
- a box that ends without its deliverable fails and waits at a gate
- a deliverable left from before does not count
- skipping a failed box goes on and stopping ends the run
- a brief not written yet is asked for again saying which file is missing
- a box whose when done waits asks before the next starts
- an idle heard before the sessions answer began does not end the box
- a box whose session ended while its watcher started again ends once the watcher is back
- the flows next run uses the server its last run started
- a gate says which try the box it sends back to runs and when it is past its tries

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner_controls.py`

Design 0013 §4, §5 and §6 (decisions 0059 and 0061): a box over its time limit, or past the tokens it may write, has its session stopped and fails; a box in Auto has its session's changes accepted while the workspace stays in Review; and the owner's controls: pause lets the running box finish and starts no other until the run goes on; stop stops the running box's session and ends the run; run from here runs the box and every box after it again, as new tries, each first message saying why it runs again; and a second flow's run waits behind the first. A nudge of the runner from another thread never waits for the event loop (§6: the runner runs in the service, which nothing may hang). Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a box over its time limit has its session stopped and fails
- a box past the tokens it may write has its session stopped and fails
- a box in auto has its changes accepted while the workspace stays in review
- pause lets the running box finish and starts no other until the run goes on
- stop stops the running boxs session and ends the run
- run from here runs the box and every box after it again as new tries
- a second flows run waits behind the first and begins when it ends
- a run stopped while its box is starting starts no session
- a nudge from another thread returns at once while the event loop is busy

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner_routes.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §10 (decisions 0061 and 0065): the runner's routes, `/flows/<workspace>/<name>/run|pause|stop` and `/runs`, each recorded with the client that asked; and a gate answered from the phone, as the phone page sends it (an option, no words), lets the flow go on. The service runs in the test (its routes, its operations, the flows' runner), against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a gate answered from the phone lets the flow go on
- the routes start pause resume stop and run from a box each recorded with its client

### `tests/components/flows/test_runs.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §8 (decision 0061): a run of a flow is a readable JSON file in the run's folder, beside each try's folder; runs of a flow are numbered from 1; the boxes run along their lines, a DECOMPOSE's tasks one after another in the order of the boxes, and what comes after a box is every box later in that order. Design 0013 §10 and §14 step 7: the phone and VS Code read the runs' files from their own threads while the runner writes them, and a write waits for a read to end (Windows refuses to replace a file open for reading) rather than failing the box; a read waits for a replace to end (Windows refuses for a moment to open a file being replaced) rather than taking the run for one that does not read.

- a run is a readable json file in its folder read back the same
- runs of a flow are numbered from one and every run is found
- a file that does not read as a run is left out and said
- a try has a folder of its own under its box
- the boxes run along their lines and a decomposes tasks in the order of the boxes
- a runs file is written while another thread reads it
- a runs file is read while another thread replaces it

### `tests/components/flows/test_stale.py`

Design 0013 §8 (decision 0061): a finished box is changed when its settings are edited, or when its deliverable in the folder no longer matches its fingerprint; every box after it that has run is then stale, and nothing runs again on its own. Progress is not a change: a TASK box that ticks its step in PLAN.md, adds its Log line and sets the plan's status marks nothing stale, even when the runner looks while the TASK still works, while a change to the plan's other lines, or a tick made between boxes, does. An edit the owner makes at a gate is the owner's: it marks nothing stale. Running again from a box, or sending one back, takes the boxes before it as they stand, so a later change to them marks the boxes after them stale again. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- an edit to a finished box marks the boxes after it stale and starts nothing
- a tasks own progress in the plan marks nothing stale
- a task that changes the plans other lines marks the boxes after the plan stale
- a tick made between boxes marks the boxes after the plan stale
- an edit approved at a gate marks nothing stale
- a finished run whose workspace is gone is passed over quietly
- a tasks progress seen while it still works marks nothing stale
- a box edited again after running from the box after it marks that box stale again
- a box edited again after a box after it was sent back marks the boxes after it stale again

### `tests/components/flows/test_verify.py`

Design 0013 §2, §8 and §9: a VERIFY ends by calling `flow_verdict`, which the service gives to the VERIFY box at work in the workspace: *pass*, or *fail* with numbered findings, and `criteria`, each line of the brief's limits and of what done means that the check touched, met or not, with its evidence. The verdict is kept on the VERIFY's try and as `verdict.md` in the try's folder; a pass goes on along the VERIFY's pass line; a VERIFY that ends without a verdict is a failed box, never a pass, and waits at a gate. A verdict is refused, saying why, while no VERIFY works in the workspace, before its try began or after it ended, and a fail without findings is refused, as is a criterion not as `flow_verdict` takes it, naming the criterion and its field; the newest verdict of a try counts, and each is an event with its actor. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a verifys pass is kept on its try and as its verdict file and the flow goes on along its pass line
- a verify that ends without a verdict fails and waits at a gate
- a verdict while no verify works in the workspace or after its try ended is refused saying why
- a fail without findings or a result but pass or fail is refused saying what to fix
- a criterion not as flow verdict takes it is refused naming the criterion and its field
- a verdict before the verifys first message was sent is refused and nothing is kept
- the newest verdict of a try counts and each is an event with its actor

### `tests/components/flows/test_verify_loop.py`

Design 0013 §3, §5 and §10 (decision 0057): a VERIFY's fail line leads back to the box it checks, which runs its next try handed the findings, and every box after it up to the VERIFY runs again; the VERIFY's own next try is handed its last findings, to check how each was answered. The box runs again only while it has tries: when they are used up, the fail goes to a gate instead, so the third fail of a box with three tries never brings a fourth. That gate offers *one more try* (past the cap, with the owner's note), *take it as it is* (the flow goes on along the pass line, the box's files as the owner left them at the gate), *skip the check* and *stop*. The driver takes the fail line from the run's file alone, so a restart between the VERIFY's end and the loop taken goes on the same way. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a fail hands the checked boxs next try the findings and the verifys next try its own
- the third fail goes to a gate never to a fourth try
- one more try at the loops gate runs the checked box past its cap with the owners note
- take it as it is goes on along the pass line with the owners edit made at the gate
- skip the check goes on and marks the verify skipped
- a restart between the verifys end and the loop taken goes on the same way

### `tests/components/flows/test_waiting.py`

Design 0013 §2, §8, §9 and §10 (decision 0061): what a flow waits for, told the same way to every client. Each question a flow's run waits for (a YOU box, a gate, a failed box, a batch of proposals) names its flow, run and box, the files it shows (each with its place in the folder, the box that wrote it and the tries of that box that kept a copy of it, so a client can diff the last two; a batch shows none), and the answers that take words; a VERIFY's loop gate shows the file of the box it checks (board D3), and one more try's words go to that box; a question answered anywhere is no longer listed. `GET /api/v1/flows/waiting` lists them. A try's copy of a deliverable is read through `GET /api/v1/flows/<workspace>/<name>/copy` as it was kept, from the try's folder only: a copy the try did not keep is refused, saying why. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a gate names its flow run and box and the files it shows with the tries that kept a copy
- a you box names the files the owner writes
- a failed box names its own deliverables and try again takes words
- a question answered anywhere is no longer listed
- the route lists what the flows wait for
- each trys copy is read as it was kept after the file in the folder moved on
- a copy the try did not keep is refused saying why
- a verify loops gate shows the checked boxs file and its tries
- a batch of proposals the run waits for names its flow run and box and shows no file

### `tests/components/gateway/test_activity.py`

Design 0004 §2: the gateway says what it does now: each request in flight with its phase (waiting for its slot, reading the prompt, answering), its time, its tokens (counted by kind: design 0012 §5.2) and its speed; and how the last request that ended went: its tokens, its time and its speed (the engine's own figures when it gives them), or why it was refused. A request the gateway answers itself (OpenCode's titles) is not shown, and one whose answer never started streaming is forgotten. Design 0012 §5.4: a request in flight shows its loop guard's newest share of each part against its ratio, and the last says what repeated when the guard stopped it. Design 0012 §5.1 and §5.3: a request in flight says its time to the first token, the kind of text it wrote last, and the call being written, its name and its arguments' newest characters. Design 0012 §3 and §7: the activity and the live text of one session give its own requests and its own last, its subagents' too only when they are asked for, never another session's; each of the newest sessions keeps its last request and its text after another session's ended, and a session's subagents never push its own last out. Design 0012 §6.1: a session's requests that ended, with the ids of the calls each answer made, are given when asked for, so the service pairs them with the session's steps. Design 0012 §8: a request whose engine broke off its answer says so once it ended, for the Watch for rule that marks it. Design 0012 §4: that same word keeps it from being taken for a finished step; each slot's newest request is the one that reached its engine (never one the gateway refused), and it is marked gone once its engine stopped, started again or was switched, so the Monitor never says an engine holds a conversation it lost. Design 0012 §9.1: a request that ended keeps its time to the first token, the measure of the engine's reading speed that the Guide's costs are read at. Design 0012 §9.4: a held request a note released, answered by the gateway itself, is never counted nor its session's last, and stays among its session's requests that ended, so its step's flow shows the hold. Design 0012 §4.2: a request's census is kept only among its session's requests that ended, whole from the newest that ended well on, its parts' numbers alone before it.

- a request in flight shows its phase its time its tokens and its speed
- a request in flight says its time to the first token what it writes now and the call being written
- the last request keeps its tokens its time and its speed and the engines own figures win
- the tokens of a request in flight are counted by kind and add up
- a request in flight shows its loop guards shares against its ratio
- a refused request is the last with its reason
- a request whose engine broke off says so once it ended
- a request that ended keeps its time to the first token
- a title answered here is not shown and a stream that never started is forgotten
- the live view gives a requests thinking answer and tool calls from an offset and keeps the last
- the live text of one session follows its own requests its subagents when asked and never anothers
- each of the newest sessions keeps its last request after another session ends one
- a sessions subagents never push its own last out
- the activity of one session gives its own requests and its own last and its subagents when asked
- a sessions requests that ended are given when asked oldest first and bounded
- a request that ended keeps its preset and the engines own usage and timings
- each slots newest request is its oldest in flight else its last that ended
- two requests in flight that began at the same moment are both shown
- a sessions requests that ended say which one its engine broke off
- a slots newest request is marked gone once its engine stopped started again or was switched
- a request a note released stays among its sessions ended but is never counted nor its last
- a sessions requests that ended keep their census whole only from the newest that ended well

### `tests/components/gateway/test_census.py`

Design 0012 §4.2, §4.3, §4.6 and §11 step 12 (decisions 0051 and 0052): the census of a model request, made in the gateway from the body it parsed: each part's characters (the system prompt, the tool definitions, the owner's messages, the model's text, the thinking sent back, the calls, the results and loop text), the thinking before the last real user message counted apart, so the template's rule can be applied where the model's facts are read; each item with its call id (a tool result's `tool_call_id`, OpenCode's call id; a message's own calls' ids), its tool and what it is; loop text found by the loop guard's measure over whole items (zlib's share under the guard's ratio, an item at least as long as its shortest stretch), the result that echoes a call that looped, and the third and later identical call in the last twelve, each taken out of the part it came from; the earlier results of a call made again with the same arguments, which could be freed; only the biggest items kept, and each part's; and a body of odd shapes counted without failing. The requests are synthetic.

- a requests characters are counted part by part
- thinking before the last user message is counted apart
- a tool result carries its call id and its tool and a message the ids of its calls
- loop text is found by the guards measure over whole items and taken out of its part
- the result of a call that looped echoes it and is loop text too
- a call made a third time in the last twelve is loop text with its result
- the earlier results of a call made again could be freed
- only the biggest items are kept and each parts biggest
- a body of odd shapes is counted without failing
- an item without a call names the calls around it
- a long chat of answers without calls is counted in time that grows with its length
- with its words off no item carries any text of the request

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cloud_bounds.py`

Design 0016 §9 and §10 (decisions 0075, 0103, 0112): the gateway checks each cloud request's workspace, by the key its OpenCode sends (its SHA-256 in the routes), before anything is sent. A request with no known key goes to this machine only, and to the cloud is refused saying to start OpenCode again; a workspace allowed the cloud goes; one not yet answered is held, its stream kept alive with comment lines while the service asks the question, and goes on on the same stream once allowed; one kept local is refused with words that say how to allow it. Notion answers only a known key whose bound is this machine, and, while no workspace may use the cloud, a request that names none too (a container started before workspaces had keys). The activity routes take the service's local token. While the service does not answer, a cloud request waits for fresh routes, then is refused `service_silent`. Through the service, a request held asks the workspace's question once.

- a workspace allowed the cloud goes
- a request with no known key is refused for the cloud
- a request with no known key is served by an engine
- an unanswered workspaces stream is held then goes on once allowed
- a workspace kept local is refused with words that say how to allow it
- a withdrawn agreement stops the next cloud request
- notion answers only a known key kept on this machine
- while no workspace may use the cloud notion answers a container started before the keys
- the activity routes take the services local token
- with the service silent a cloud request waits then is refused service silent
- a held request asks the workspaces question through the service
- the service asks the gateways activity with its token

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cloud_cost.py`

Design 0016 §12 (decision 0108): each cloud request's cost is OpenRouter's own figure, its stream's or whole answer's `usage.cost` (`how`: `usage`), kept in its record and appended to the gateway's spool with its tokens, its generation, its session and workspace; a stream cut short has none, so it is spooled as an estimate (the tokens counted at the model's prices) and made exact from the generation's `total_cost` when the service asks for it afterward (`how`: `generation`); a refused request is spooled with its status and code, at no cost. The service takes the spool every two seconds into its own table, by the spool's run and sequence, so a restart of the gateway or a take repeated counts nothing twice; the spool's route takes only the service's token.

- a streams usage cost is its cost spooled with its tokens and generation
- a whole answers cost too
- a stream cut short is spooled as an estimate and a refusal with its code
- the spool survives a restart and forgets only what was taken
- the service takes the spool once and makes an estimate exact from the generation

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cloud_forwarding.py`

Design 0016 §8 (decisions 0109 and 0110): the gateway forwards a cloud slot's requests to the provider's `/chat/completions` with the listed model id, the bearer key it holds, the session for the provider's cache and the record's data policy, nothing else changed; a stream comes back line by line, the provider's comment lines and tool calls' pieces unchanged, a whole answer whole; a mid-stream error ends the stream; a request is never sent again, a connection refused answered 502 `cloud_unreachable` and one broken off ending the stream; an answer silent for the read timeout ends with `cloud_silent`; and each refusal passes with its status and Clank's words of what to do, never OpenCode's retry words for those it must not retry, and never a status it retries by: no provider under the record's data policy (OpenRouter's 503) is answered 403 (decision 0115). A key echoed in an answer never reaches the record or the live view.

- a streamed request reaches the provider as the tool sent it with the key and the session
- a whole answer comes back whole
- a tool calls pieces and thinking pass unchanged
- a mid stream error ends the stream and is recorded
- a connection refused is answered cloud unreachable and never sent again
- a stream broken off before any answer ends and is never sent again
- an answer silent for the read timeout ends with cloud silent
- each refusal passes with its status and words of what to do
- a key echoed in an answer never reaches the record or the live view

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cloud_gate.py`

Design 0016 §9, §10 and §11 (decisions 0103, 0111 and 0112): every path to the provider passes one gate, right before the request is sent, whatever held it first. A paused session's request, resumed while a cloud model serves, is refused for a workspace kept on this machine, kept from the cloud when its session used Notion, and sent without Clank's Notion tools when it may go; a request held at a spending limit is looked at again once the limit is raised, so a workspace kept on this machine meanwhile, or a session marked local only meanwhile, never reaches the provider. A refusal told inside a held stream says what held it (a pause, the limit, the workspace's question), and is worded to be retried when nothing was sent (the provider unreachable). The sessions, pages and words here are made up.

- a paused request resumed for a workspace kept on this machine never reaches the provider
- a paused request resumed whose session read notion is kept from the cloud
- a paused request resumed that may go is sent without clanks notion tools
- a request held at the limit for a workspace kept local meanwhile never reaches the provider
- a request held at the limit whose session was marked local meanwhile never reaches the provider
- a refusal inside a stream held at the limit says so and unreachable is to be retried
- a refusal inside a stream held for a pause says its session was paused

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cloud_keys.py`

Design 0016 §5 (decision 0106): the service hands the gateway a cloud provider's key, behind its local token, while a cloud model is loaded. The gateway's `/keys` routes take only the service's token and never answer a value; a load hands the key once, read through the kernel by `gateway` for its launch; a new key set while loaded replaces it; an unload takes it back; a gateway started again gets it at the next look; a request waits while the key is not there and goes once it is; and a key that appears in a request is replaced in its record by `…` and its last four.

- the keys routes take only the services token
- the gateway holds a key in memory and never answers its value
- a load hands the key once a new key replaces it and an unload takes it back
- a gateway started again gets the key at the next look
- a request for a cloud slot waits while its key is not there and goes once it is
- a key in a request is replaced in its record

### `tests/components/gateway/test_completion.py`

Design 0006 §4: the gateway's fill in the middle. A completion for the `completion` role goes to the fast slot's engine (llama.cpp's `/infill`), its fields as they came but for the model and with time limits a late completion needs; the newest completion wins (the one in flight is broken off, its client answered with nothing); with no engine serving the role it is refused at once, never held; and the gateway keeps the last ones in memory, with their times, for the Completion page, never in its records of the agent's requests.

- a completion goes to the fast slots engine and is counted
- the newest completion wins and the one in flight is broken off
- with no engine for the role a completion is refused at once

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cost_limits.py`

Design 0016 §11 (decisions 0104 and 0108): the optional daily and monthly limits pause the cloud. At 80% of a limit the owner is told once; at the limit, each new cloud request is held before it is sent (a stream kept alive with comment lines), and the owner is asked once whether to raise it: *double* raises it and the held stream goes on, *raise* takes an amount within its bounds (at least a cent above what was spent, so a raise always lets the cloud go on), *keep* leaves it held until the day ends; a raise that still leaves the spending at the limit asks again, with the new numbers; requests in flight finish; a local request is never held; the gateway counts the requests the service has not taken yet, so a burst between two takes is counted.

- at the limit a cloud request is held and goes on once raised
- kept paused it is held until the day ends
- requests in flight finish and a local request is never held
- a burst between two takes is counted
- at eighty percent the owner is told once
- at the limit the owner is asked once and double raises it
- a raise that still leaves the spending at the limit asks again
- raise takes an amount and keep waits for the day to end

### `tests/components/gateway/test_cost_views.py`

Design 0016 §11 and §12 (decisions 0104 and 0108): what the cloud cost, as every client reads it from the service and none recomputes. Today and this month are this computer's local days, the periods' bounds given with their words (a day the clocks change is 25 or 23 hours long, its bounds each at the offset of its own moment); a session's cost counts its subagents'; a flow's is its boxes' sessions'; an estimate shows apart (≈) until it is exact; the runtime's own requests (a load's health, a check, a test) are counted in the totals; a workspace's own today and this month are given beside them when asked (`?workspace=`), the limits staying the whole cloud's; the lists take a revision and give only what changed since; and the limits are written to `config/cost.json`, recorded with who set them.

- today and this month are this computers days across midnight
- a day the clocks change runs from its own midnight to the next
- in the systems own zone each bound is at the offset of its own moment
- an estimate shows apart until it is exact and the runtimes own requests count
- a workspaces own cost is given beside the whole clouds when asked
- a sessions cost counts its subagents and the lists give only what changed
- a flows cost is its boxes sessions
- the limits are written to their file and recorded

### `tests/components/gateway/test_engine_errors.py`

Design 0004 §2 (found by the model battery, 2026-10-15): an engine that fails mid-answer (KVMem after a 68K-token prompt: "multimodal query replay failed") sends an error event whose `error` is a plain string, which OpenAI's clients (OpenCode's AI SDK) refuse to read ("expected object, received string"), so the user saw a parse error, not the reason. The gateway passes an error event on as OpenAI writes one (an object with its message, type and code) and records the error, so the Requests page shows the request failed and why.

- an engines error given as a string is passed on as openai writes one and recorded
- an error already as an object is kept and recorded and other lines pass untouched

### `tests/components/gateway/test_flows_forwarding.py`

Design 0013 §9 and §15 (decisions 0020, 0064 and 0097): a box's model reaches its flow through the gateway, which forwards to the service only what the agent may ask (reading its flow, `GET /flows/read`; proposing a box and submitting its proposals, `POST /flows/propose` and `/flows/submit`; a VERIFY's verdict, `POST /flows/verdict`; a planning's draft of a whole flow, `POST /flows/draft`, design 0013 §15) as the client `agent`, and refuses everything else under `/flows/` (403), forwarding none of it: running, pausing or stopping a flow, its runs, checking it, changing it, deciding proposals, and opening a planning. A refusal of the service comes back with its words, and a service that does not answer is said so. End to end, a verdict given through the gateway is kept as the agent's session's, a criterion not as `flow_verdict` takes it is refused naming its field, and a flow cannot be run, paused or stopped through the gateway.

- the agents verdict is forwarded and nothing else under flows
- a refusal comes back with its words and a silent service is said so
- a verdict through the gateway is the agents and the agent cannot run pause or stop a flow

### `tests/components/gateway/test_full_context.py`

Design 0012 §4.1 (revised 2026-10-20, decision 0090) and design 0004 §2: an engine's refusal of a request its context cannot hold (KVMem's 400, "prompt + max_tokens exceeds n_ctx", which OpenCode 1.19 takes for an ordinary error and stops on; upstream llama.cpp's 400, `exceed_context_size_error`) is answered by the gateway in words and a code OpenCode reads as a context overflow, on which it compacts the session and goes on: a 400 in OpenAI's shape whose message says the context was full and that the request exceeds the available context size, its code `context_length_exceeded`, streamed or not; and inside a stream whose 200 has gone out (a held request, after its resume), an error event in the one shape OpenCode reads there. The record marks it (`full`, the engine's words), and so do the session's requests that ended. Any other refusal is passed on as the engine gave it, its error recorded. The requests are synthetic.

- kvmems refusal of a full context is answered as an overflow opencode compacts on
- upstream llamacpps refusal of a full context is answered the same way
- any other refusal is passed on as the engine gave it and recorded
- a held requests full context is told in the stream in the one shape opencode reads there

### `tests/components/gateway/test_hold_gateway.py`

Design 0012 §9.4 and §11 step 11 (decision 0055), through a gateway served in the test and the fake engine: a paused session's request gets its headers at once and comment lines while it is held, and reaches no engine; resumed, it goes to the engine on the same stream; the activity shows it held and its record keeps the hold; a note's release closes only the requests held before the note was sent, and one it closed stays among its session's ended requests for its step's flow; a session not paused is not held; and the routes that pause and resume take only the service's local token (decision 0084), so the agent, which reaches the gateway through the edge, never can. The keep-alive is short here (50 ms) so a test sees several; the sessions are synthetic.

- a held request gets its headers at once and comment lines while held and reaches no engine
- resuming forwards the held request on the same stream
- a held request shows as held in the activity and its record keeps the hold
- a request of a session not paused is not held
- the holds routes refuse a caller without the services token
- a subagent is held with its parent
- a note closes the hold with an empty step and the next request is held again
- a release leaves held a request that came after the time it names
- a request not streamed is held without headers until resumed
- a held request whose client left is forgotten and reaches no engine
- words not to be retried hold none of opencodes retry words
- a slot that is not served after resuming is an error event worded to be retried
- an engines own refusal after resuming is worded so opencode does not retry it
- the wait for a starting slot after resuming keeps the comment lines

### `tests/components/gateway/test_holds.py`

Design 0012 §9.4 and §11 step 11 (decision 0055): the gateway's holds. A paused session's requests are held, and its subagents' at any depth (a request names only its direct parent, so the gateway keeps the parents it heard); a held request is told so at once and then kept alive with a comment line within every keep-alive, well under the 300 s OpenCode waits without bytes, until the owner resumes; a note's release closes only the session's own requests held before the note was sent, never the one that carries it, and the pause stays; and the pauses outlive the gateway's restart, in their file. The clock and the wait are fakes; the sessions are synthetic.

- a paused session holds its own requests and its subagents at any depth
- a held request gets a comment line at once and one within every keep alive until resumed
- a release closes only the requests of the session itself and the pause stays
- a release never closes a request held after the message was sent
- pausing twice keeps the first pause and resuming one not paused says so
- a request resumed under another pause stays held by it
- pauses are kept in their file and read again at the next start

### `tests/components/gateway/test_local_only.py`

Design 0016 §10 (decision 0111): local-only content never reaches the cloud model. Before a request goes to the cloud, the gateway removes from its tools Clank's Notion tools and each tool of a server marked local (as OpenCode names them), saying so in its notes; a request whose messages call one is refused (403 `local_only`), and so is every later request of its session, and of the subagents it starts, even once a compaction folded the call away: the gateway marks a session the first time any of its requests shows such a call, whatever slot serves it, and keeps the marks in `state/local-only.json`, so they survive a restart. A local model is given every tool, and a new session goes to the cloud. Clank's Notion tools are kept from the cloud by the gateway itself, whatever the routes name; and while the service says the tools kept from the cloud were not given yet, no request goes to the cloud (503, to be retried).

- local only tools are removed from a cloud request and kept for a local one
- a session that used a local tool is refused the cloud even after a compaction
- clanks notion tools are kept from the cloud whatever the routes name
- no request goes to the cloud while the tools kept from it were not given
- a call of a local servers tool names the server
- the marks survive a restart of the gateway

### `tests/components/gateway/test_loops.py`

Design 0004 §2 (the loop guard; decision 0040): an answer's part that repeats itself is found soon after its newest stretch fills (closing tags again and again, a number written again and again), a paragraph of prose said again by the long stretch only; a tool call's arguments are read on the short stretch only, so a file written from a template is not taken for a loop; Clank's own code, documents and most repetitive file never are; each tool call is read apart from the others; the share and the stretches are its settings; the words say which part repeated, over which stretch and how far it compresses, and its brief words (the ones an agent is sent) carry no numbers, since an agent may retry an error by the numbers in its words; and the guard trips once. Design 0012 §5.4: it keeps each part's newest reading, the lowest share and over which stretch, for the Monitor to show against its ratio, and lists each part it was fed in the order it was first fed, one not yet as long as its shortest stretch without a share.

- closing tags said again and again are found soon after the short stretch fills
- a thinking that writes the same number again and again is found
- a paragraph of prose said again and again is found by the long stretch only
- a file a tool call writes from a template is not taken for a loop
- clanks own code documents and its most repetitive file are never taken for a loop
- each tool call is read apart from the others
- the share and the stretches are its settings
- the words say which part repeated over which stretch and how far it compresses
- its brief words carry no numbers so no agent retries by them
- the guard trips once and then keeps what it found
- the guard keeps each parts newest share and its stretch
- a part fed first but not yet as long as its shortest stretch is listed first without a share

### `tests/components/gateway/test_notion_forwarding.py`

Design 0010 §3: the agent reaches Notion through the gateway, which forwards to the service the reading and proposing routes only (the shared databases, a query, a page, a search; a new entry, a change, a removal, submitting the batch) as the client `agent`, and refuses every other (approving, rejecting, connecting, the status, the batches); a refusal of the service comes back with its words, and a service that does not answer is said so. End to end, a proposal made through the gateway is staged as the agent's session's, and the agent cannot approve it.

- the agents reading and proposing are forwarded and nothing else
- a refusal comes back with its words and a silent service is said so
- a proposal through the gateway is the agents and the agent cannot approve it

### `tests/components/gateway/test_reasoning_field.py`

Design 0016 §8 and design 0015 §8 (decision 0074): thinking streamed as `reasoning`, as OpenRouter streams it, is read as `reasoning_content` is: it shows in the live view, counts as thinking, trips the loop guard when it repeats itself, and reaches the tool unchanged; a delta that carries both is read once.

- thinking as reasoning counts as thinking and is heard
- a delta carrying both names is read once
- thinking as reasoning that repeats itself trips the loop guard
- thinking as reasoning shows in the live view and reaches the tool unchanged

### `tests/components/gateway/test_recent_requests.py`

Design 0001 §6 (the Requests page, 2026-10-15): the gateway's kept requests are read back newest first, across the days' files and from each file's end, each in brief (who asked, its status, its tokens in, cached and out, its first token, total time and speed, the start of what was asked); one is found by its id with what was asked (the last message the user wrote) and what was answered. A line still being written is skipped. Design 0004 §2: a request the loop guard stopped says so in brief. Design 0012 §3: a record in brief names its session and its parent, from the headers it kept. Design 0003 §4.1: a request found by its id says the sampling it sent of its own (its temperature, top_p and the rest), none when it sent none, and not known when the gateway kept no copy of it.

- the newest requests come first across days each in brief
- a request the loop guard stopped says so in brief
- a long day is read from its end in pieces
- one request is found with what was asked and answered
- a record in brief names its session and its parent
- a request found says the sampling it sent of its own

### `tests/components/gateway/test_records.py`

Design 0001 §6.5: the gateway keeps one JSON line per request, in one file per day under `logs/traffic/`; a field longer than the limit is cut, saying how long it was; a request's credentials never reach the file; a day's file older than the days kept is removed.

- one line per request in a file per day
- a long field is cut saying how long it was and credentials never reach the file
- a days file older than the days kept is removed

### `tests/components/gateway/test_server.py`

Design 0001 §6.1-§6.5, design 0004 §2 and design 0016 §8: the gateway sends a request for a role to the engine of the role's slot, with the model name the runtime's settings give and in that engine's own fields (KVMem takes llama.cpp's); a streamed answer streams; with no engine running the request is refused, saying how to load one; a request that comes while the slot switches waits for the new engine; a client that leaves mid-answer makes the engine stop; OpenCode's title requests are answered by the gateway itself; `/v1/models` lists the roles; and every request is recorded, its credentials never. An engine that breaks off a request before answering anything (it crashed) has the request sent again once its slot is served again (the runtime restarts it), on the same stream; one that breaks off after answering, or whose slot does not come back, ends the stream with an error that says so, and so does one whose slot was switched to a cloud model meanwhile: what was meant for this machine is never sent to the cloud by the gateway itself. A stream that stops without its end (no finish reason, no `[DONE]`) has broken off too: on Linux a crashed engine's connection is closed cleanly, so nothing else shows it. The loop guard (design 0004 §2, decision 0040): an answer or a tool call that repeats itself is stopped soon, the engine stops writing it, and the stream ends with an error that says what repeated; with the guard off it runs to its end.

- a request reaches the slots engine with its model name and in its own fields
- a streamed answer streams and its usage and first token are recorded
- the live view follows a streamed answer as it comes and keeps it after
- with no engine running the request is refused saying how to load one
- a request that comes while the slot switches waits for the new engine
- a client that leaves mid answer makes the engine stop
- opencodes title request is answered by the gateway
- models lists the roles
- the gateway says what it does now and how the last request went
- a request the engine broke off before answering is sent again once its slot is back
- a request the engine broke off while its slot switched to a cloud model ends saying so
- an engine that breaks off after answering ends the stream with an error that says so
- a slot that does not come back ends the stream with an error
- a stream that stops without its end after answering ends with an error that says so
- a stream that stops without its end before answering is sent again once its slot is back
- a client that leaves an answer sent again is kept as having left
- a whole answer the engine broke off is asked again once its slot is back
- an answer that repeats itself is stopped and the stream ends with an error that says so
- a tool call that repeats itself is stopped too
- a thinking that repeats itself is stopped too
- each tool call of an answer is read apart by its index
- the gateway reads the stretches its settings give
- with the loop guard off an answer that repeats itself runs to its end

### `tests/components/gateway/test_service.py`

Design 0004 §2 and design 0001 §6.6: the service tells the gateway its routes (the main slot's state, whether an operation is changing it, and while an engine runs, its port, its serving setup and version, the model name it answers to, its preset and the preset's context and output limits, and, design 0012 §4 and decision 0067, the preset's facts, its engine's and its model's; the role `agent` is the main slot's); it starts the gateway's process, and after a restart of the service it adopts the one still running instead of starting another; `clank gateway status` says where it listens and which process it is, and `GET /gateway/activity` what it does now. Design 0012 §7: `GET /gateway/activity` and `GET /gateway/live` follow the session they are asked for, and its subagents too when `subagents` asks for them. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): the service pauses and resumes a session in the gateway with its local token, and `GET /gateway/holds` says what is paused and held; a gateway from before pausing, still running, is never taken for one that paused.

- the routes give the main slots engine its model name and its limits
- a slot an operation is changing is busy
- the service starts the gateway adopts it after a restart and says what it does
- the services activity and live text follow one session
- the service pauses and resumes a session in the gateway with its token
- a gateway from before pausing is never taken for one that paused

### `tests/components/gateway/test_session_facts.py`

Design 0012 §3 and §11 step 2: the gateway names each model request's session, and for a subagent's request its parent, from the headers OpenCode sends with it (`X-Session-Id`, `x-parent-session-id`, read in any case), in its activity (each request in flight, and the last that ended) and in its records in brief; a request that names no session works as before and names none. Design 0012 §5.2: the tokens a streamed answer brings are counted by kind, its thinking, its text and its tool calls. Design 0012 §5.4: a request in flight shows its loop guard's newest share of each part it reads. Design 0012 §7: the gateway's activity and live view follow the session they are asked for. Design 0012 §4.1 (§11 step 3): a session's request that ended keeps the engine's own usage and timings, so each figure is read where that engine reports it. Design 0012 §4.2 (§11 step 12): the gateway makes the census of each request from its body and keeps it with the session's requests that ended, never in its records. The sessions here are synthetic.

- a session and its parent are read from opencodes headers in any case
- a request with a session id shows its session in flight when it ended and in its record
- a subagents request shows its parent
- a request without the header still works and names no session
- the gateway counts a streamed answers thinking and its text apart
- the gateway counts a streamed tool calls tokens as tool while it streams and at its end
- the gateway shows the loop guards newest share of a streamed answer
- the live view of the gateway follows the session it is asked for
- the gateway keeps the ids of the calls an answer made
- each fake engine style reports its cached tokens in its own field through the gateway
- the gateway makes a census of each request and keeps it out of its records
- with records off the census the gateway keeps carries no text of the request
- a census that fails never fails the request and is logged as a count without its text

### `tests/components/gateway/test_titles.py`

Design 0001 §6.4 and design 0004 §2: OpenCode asks for a session's title in a small request of its own just before the session's first prompt; answered by the model, that request would take the place of the conversation the engine keeps cached (measured with Clank's prototype: 52 of 89 long re-reads, the desktop freezing during some), so the gateway answers it itself, from the first line of the user's message, cut at a clause, then at a word; a request with tools, or with another first message, is not one.

- opencodes title request is answered from the first line of the users message
- a long first line is cut at its first clause then at a word
- a request with tools or another first message is not a title request

### `tests/components/gateway/test_vision.py`

Design 0008 §7: the model that reads images, in the vision slot on the CPU, kept as its mode says. On demand (the default), a request for the role `vision` makes the service load it (the gateway asks first, then waits for the load) and it is unloaded after its idle minutes without use; always, it is loaded and kept; off, nothing loads it, a request is refused saying how to turn it on, and the agent's model is told it reads no images. The routes say each role's demand; the vision view says the mode, the slot and how long since it was wanted.

- on demand a request loads it and idle unloads it
- always loads and keeps it and off refuses and unloads

### `tests/components/gateway/test_vision_gateway.py`

Design 0008 §7, in the gateway's process: a request for a role loaded on demand (the vision model) makes the gateway ask the service for it first, then wait while it loads, and the request is served; when the service says why not (vision is off), the request is refused in those words. A role not loaded on demand is never asked for.

- a request for the vision model has it loaded then is served
- with vision off the request is refused saying how to turn it on

### `tests/components/gateway/test_workspace_keys.py`

Design 0016 §9 (decision 0075's keys, built for the cloud): each workspace has a key of its own at the gateway, made once by the gateway's service and kept with the secrets (`workspace/<name>/gateway-key`), its SHA-256 kept with the workspace's bound in the gateway's own state; the routes the gateway follows carry each key's hash with its workspace, its bound, whether it was answered and its open question, never the key; a bound changed is in the next routes. A key is made only once the cloud is in play (a provider agreed, or the workspace allowed the cloud): before, a workspace's agent is given `local` as before, and nothing is made or read among the secrets.

- before the cloud is in play no key is made
- a workspaces key is made once and kept with the secrets
- the routes carry each keys hash and bound never the key
- a bound changed is in the next routes

### `tests/components/machine/test_checks.py`

Design 0001 §4.7 and §7.3: `clank doctor`'s checks pass, warn or fail as the machine's facts say, each with what to do about it, and the checks that belong to one platform are skipped on the others.

- a well set up machine passes every check
- the database check closes the database it read
- a machine without a gpu wsl or docker gets warnings with what to do
- hardware accelerated gpu scheduling turned on is a warning
- windows checks are skipped elsewhere
- a clank home that cannot be written fails
- little room for models is a warning and room that cannot be measured too

### `tests/components/machine/test_detect.py`

Design 0001 §2 and §7.3: Clank detects the machine it runs on (system, Python, WSL, processor, memory, GPUs, Docker) from the system's own commands, and says plainly when there is no GPU, no WSL or no Docker.

- gpus are read from nvidia smi
- the default wsl distribution and its version are read
- detection gathers every fact from the commands
- without a gpu wsl or docker detection says so
- wsl is not asked about outside windows

### `tests/components/machine/test_doctor.py`

Design 0001 §7.3 and design 0002 §3.2 (M0.3): `clank doctor` prints each check and how many passed, as text or JSON, and works without the service; inside the service, the doctor is an operation that records the machine profile and the checks.

- doctor prints each check and how many passed
- doctor prints json for scripts and models
- the doctor operation records the profile and the checks

### `tests/components/machine/test_meters.py`

Design 0004 §9 (the owner's request, 2026-10-14: the console shows the machine's CPU, GPU, memory, disks and network all the time). The service reads them once a second: each reading has the CPU's use (as a whole and per logical processor), the memory and page file in use, the disks' and the network's rates since the reading before, each fixed drive's space and each NVIDIA GPU's use, memory, temperature and power (none without NVIDIA's library); the last readings are kept, the oldest dropped past five minutes, and answered at `GET /machine/meters`; each new one is sent to the clients live.

- a reading has the cpu the memory the disks the network and the gpus
- a reading has the busiest and the largest processes read every few seconds
- the workspaces containers are read from docker stats on a thread of their own
- without nvidias library there are no gpus
- an nvidia gpu is read from its driver
- the last readings are kept and answered oldest first

### `tests/components/notion/test_applying.py`

Design 0010 §8: an approved batch is written to Notion in order: new entries first (so a relation to one proposed in the same batch points to it once written), then changes (their properties, then their page: a block changed in place, removed, or added where it belongs), then removals (to Notion's trash). An entry edited in Notion since it was staged is not written, and said so. Each proposal's result is kept: a batch stopped halfway shows what was written, and approving it again writes the rest, never a proposal twice. Some proposals may be approved and the rest left staged; a batch may be rejected with a reason. Only a submitted batch is approved, never by the agent itself. Writing is recorded without content. `clank notion batches|show|approve|reject` do the same from a terminal.

- an approved batch is written in order new entries first and relations point to them
- an entry edited in notion since it was staged is not written and said so
- a batch stopped halfway shows what was written and approving again writes the rest once
- some proposals are approved and the rest stay staged
- a batch is rejected with a reason and nothing is written
- only a submitted batch is approved and never by the agent
- writing is recorded without content
- clank notion lists shows approves and rejects batches

### `tests/components/notion/test_comments.py`

Design 0010 §7: the owner comments on a batch, on a proposal, on a property of it or on a block of its page (never the agent), and takes back a comment not sent yet; sending the batch back marks it returned and tells the agent, in one message to the session that submitted it, each comment with what it is about, and what to do. The agent revises the returned batch with the same tools: a proposal for the same entry (or a new entry of the same title) replaces the one in the batch, keeping its number and what it was (one that is written in part is never replaced); it answers each comment (`reply`), and submitting makes the batch's next revision. No comment's words are in an event.

- the owner comments on the batch a proposal a property and a block and the agent cannot
- sending back tells the agent each comment with what it is about
- a batch whose session is gone goes to a new session
- the agent revises a returned batch and answers its comments
- a proposal written in part is never replaced
- the routes take comments and a send back from the owner and replies from the agent
- clank notion comments and sends a batch back

### `tests/components/notion/test_connection.py`

Design 0010 §2-§3: Clank connects to Notion with the owner's integration secret, kept as a secret only once Notion accepts it, and never in a record, an event or an answer; the status names the integration and its workspace; every request names Notion's API version and carries the secret as a bearer token; a request Notion limits is sent again after the wait Notion asks for. `clank notion connect` asks for the secret without showing it.

- connecting keeps the secret only once notion accepts it and the status names what it reached
- every request names notions api version and carries the secret as a bearer token
- a request notion limits is sent again after the wait notion asks for
- the secret is in no record event or answer
- disconnecting removes the secret and the status says how to connect
- clank notion connect asks for the secret without showing it and says what it reached

### `tests/components/notion/test_markdown.py`

Design 0010 §4: an entry's page reaches the agent as Markdown: headings, paragraphs with their bold, italic, code, links and equations, lists with their nesting, to-dos, quotes, callouts, code, block equations, dividers, images as `notion:` links with their caption, links to other entries as `[title](notion:id)`; a block Clank cannot show is said, never dropped. A property's value reaches it as plain text.

- rich text keeps bold italic code strikethrough links equations and mentions
- a page reads as markdown with its lists nested and every block kept
- a propertys value reads as plain text

### `tests/components/notion/test_reading.py`

Design 0010 §4: the agent reads the databases shared with Clank's integration: each with its properties and their types (a select's options, the database a relation points to); a database's entries as compact rows (a relation as the titles it points to, the trash left out), filtered by a title's words or by Notion's own filter, a page at a time with a cursor; an entry's properties and its page as Markdown, read whole however many requests it takes; a search by title. What is not shared, or unknown, is said so, and so is a Clank not connected. A read is recorded with what was asked, never with what Notion answered.

- the shared databases are listed with their properties types options and what a relation points to
- a databases entries are compact rows with relations as titles and the trash left out
- entries are filtered by a titles words or by notions own filter a page at a time
- an entrys page reads as markdown with its properties
- a long page is read whole however many requests it takes
- a search finds entries and databases by title and never the trash
- what is not shared and a clank not connected are said so
- a read is recorded with what was asked and never what notion answered
- clank notion databases lists what clank can see for the owner to check their sharing
- the routes answer what the agent reads

### `tests/components/notion/test_review_service.py`

Design 0010 §6 and §8, what VS Code's review needs of the service: a proposal approved in part (some of its fields, or its page) writes only those, and the rest stays staged until approved later, written then as a change to the entry (a new entry needs its title to be made); each database of a batch as a table: its columns, the entries the batch changes (each changed cell with what it was), removes and adds, and, around them, the entries it leaves as they are (their neighbors, or all of them when asked).

- a change approved in part writes those parts and the rest later
- a new entry approved in part is made with those and the rest written to it later
- an entry edited in notion between two parts is not written again
- each database of a batch is a table of its changes and their neighbors
- the routes give the tables and approve parts

### `tests/components/notion/test_staging.py`

Design 0010 §5: the agent proposes a new entry, a change or a removal; each proposal is checked against its database's properties when staged and refused with the reason the agent can fix (a property that is not there, a value that does not fit, a relation to an entry that cannot be found, numbers without their sources, an image or a block Clank cannot make yet); it is kept with the entry as it was (its properties, its page and when it was edited), a change with its fields and its page compared block by block, in the workspace's open batch, which `submit` makes ready for review. A relation may point to an entry proposed in the same batch. Nothing is written to Notion. Batches are files in Clank's state, read again after a restart; staging is recorded without content.

- a new entry is staged in the workspaces open batch checked against its database
- a proposal that does not fit is refused with the reason
- a change keeps the entry as it was with its fields and its page compared
- a relation may point to an entry proposed in the same batch
- a removal names a shared entry and why
- submitting makes the batch ready and the next proposal opens another
- batches are read again after a restart and staging is recorded without content
- the routes stage submit and list

### `tests/components/notion/test_writing.py`

Design 0010 §5: the page the agent proposes, in Markdown, becomes Notion's blocks; read back it is the same Markdown, so a page the agent did not change compares equal. Inline Markdown becomes rich text and back. A value the agent gives for a property becomes Notion's own (a number, an option, a date, related entries), refused with the reason when it does not fit the property. A page proposed against the page now is a list of block changes: kept, removed, added after a block, or changed in place when the block keeps its kind.

- markdown becomes blocks that read back as the same markdown
- inline markdown becomes rich text and back
- a value becomes notions own and a new option is said
- a value that does not fit its property is refused with the reason
- a proposed page is compared with the page now block by block

### `tests/components/remote/test_phone.py`

Design 0005 §4 (decision 0012): who may reach the phone page, pairing, and what a paired device sees and does.

- the page answers only through tailscale to this computers own user
- tailscales forwarded address does not hide that it connected from this machine
- a device pairs once with a code from the computer and then is known
- a pairing code canceled on the computer no longer pairs
- wrong codes are counted and an expired code does not pair
- a paired device sees the model the workspaces and the files sent
- every exchange with a device is kept whole and shown to it and to the computer
- tailscale says this machines name and user or why not
- remote access is off until turned on and a code comes with the pages address

### `tests/components/remote/test_phone_flows.py`

Design 0013 §9, §10 and §14 step 7 (design 0005 §4, design 0002 §2.5): gates and proposals on the phone. The phone lists a question a flow waits for under its flow, with its box and the files it shows; reads a file it shows as it stands in the folder (what an approval takes), and only those, never through a link that leads out of the folder; and answers it, with a note for the answers that take words (send back's), the note reaching the box's next first message and the answer recorded as the device's. A batch of proposals asked about a flow, as step 4 asks it, is listed under its flow and answered there. A question is answered once, from any client: one answered in VS Code leaves the phone's list and the phone's answer is refused, naming who answered; one answered on the phone leaves the service's waiting questions and what the flows wait for. The page's script draws a flow's question with its files and its note. While no question is about a flow, the phone's overview reads no run's file. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- the phone lists a gate under its flow with its box and the files it shows
- the phone reads a file a gate shows as it stands in the folder and only those
- send back from the phone carries its note to the boxs next first message
- a batch of proposals asked about a flow is listed under its flow and answered there
- a gate answered in vscode leaves the phones list and the phones answer is refused naming who
- a gate answered on the phone leaves the services waiting questions and what the flows wait for
- the phone page draws a flows question with its files and its note
- a file a gate shows that leads out of the folder is not read
- the phones overview reads no runs file while no question is about a flow

### `tests/components/remote/test_phone_marks.py`

Design 0012 §8 (decision 0053) and design 0005 §6: a Watch for rule set to tell the phone lists its mark on the phone page, with what waits, until the mark is seen, from the phone or from the computer; seen from the phone, it is recorded as the device's. A mark of an ask the page already shows in OpenCode's own box (a permission, a question) is not listed again. The page's script draws the overview's marks with what waits and marks one seen.

- a mark told to the phone is listed with what waits until it is seen
- a mark seen on the computer leaves the phones list
- an ask shown in opencodes own box is not listed again as a mark
- the phone page draws a mark with what waits

### `tests/components/remote/test_phone_page.py`

Design 0005 §5 (transparency): on the console's Phone page, Enter on a paired device opens everything it and this computer exchanged, newest first; the highlighted exchange shows what the device sent and what it was sent back. Design 0011 §7: the window's list is a panel whose note counts the exchanges and what was sent to the device.

- enter on a device shows what it and this computer exchanged

### `tests/components/runtime/test_adopt.py`

Design 0003 §7.1 and the M1.4 plan's review focus 3: an engine already built is adopted where it is (nothing fetched, built or moved) once Clank has checked it: its program is there, its `--help` lists every flag its adapter can give, and the checkout it was built from is at the commit the adapter's version names. Another commit, or a missing flag, is refused saying what differs; local changes in the checkout are recorded with the install.

- an engine at the known commit listing every flag is adopted where it is
- paths given from the home folder are checked and recorded as the host expands them
- local changes in the checkout are recorded with the install
- an engine built from another commit is refused saying which
- a folder that is no checkout is refused asking for the source
- a program whose help lacks a flag is refused naming it
- a program that is not there is refused

### `tests/components/runtime/test_catalog.py`

Design 0003 §3 and §7.3, design 0001 §2 and §3.6: the catalog lists the models Clank knows, each file in each format with where it comes from, its size and its checksum, and the sizes each engine's memory estimate needs; each preset Clank ships names a serving setup, a version and a model it knows, with valid options; and a preset whose engine cannot read any of its model's files, or that names what Clank does not know, is refused before any plan, each problem in a sentence.

- the catalog has qwen with its vision projector with sources sizes and checksums
- the catalog has swift 1 5 with its mtp head and no projector
- qwen and swift take the same memory on kvmem but swift has no projector
- the catalog has swift 1 5 iq3 xxs which takes 1 6 gb less of the gpu
- each preset clank ships is known and valid
- a preset its engine cannot run is refused in sentences
- a model without a file its engine reads is refused

### `tests/components/runtime/test_check.py`

Design 0003 §6.5, design 0001 §3.6 and §3.8: a preset's check starts nothing and says, item by item, whether it can run: the preset is valid; its engine is installed on a host; the program is there; the model's files are there at their size; the engine's own `--help` lists every flag its plan gives it; and the plan fits in the GPU's free memory. Each item that fails says why; a GPU that cannot be read leaves the fit unknown.

- a preset that can run passes every item and nothing starts
- vision adds the projector to what is checked
- a preset without vision checks no projector
- each item that fails says why and the check stops where it cannot go on
- a flag its engine does not list fails the check naming it

### `tests/components/runtime/test_cloud_facts.py`

Design 0016 §13 (design 0012 §4, decision 0067): the Monitor reads a cloud model's figures from its preset's facts, as it reads an engine's: its window (the smaller of the preset's and the model's) and its most output; no thinking budget, no exact window, not on this machine's GPU, no token count, no one conversation; its prompt, output and cache figures read from OpenRouter's usage; where the facts were read; the context totals of a step it answered say it sits in the cloud (`gpu.device`), all of it attended. The model's facts have no template and no calibration, so the estimate shares the census out to OpenRouter's `prompt_tokens` at Clank's plain default, saying so. With records off, the census of a cloud request keeps no text of the prompt (decision 0096).

- a cloud presets facts say its window and what does not apply
