### `tests/console/test_panel.py`

Design 0011 §4 (the panel): every section is a panel, a box with its title set in its top border at the left, bold, and its note at the right of that border, drawn again when a page changes it, and dropped whole, never cut, before the title goes when the panel is narrow; a panel is never narrower than its title; the panel that holds the focus, and only it, draws its border in `border-focused`, and Tab goes from panel to panel in reading order; panels in a row share the row's height and stack when the row is narrow, or at once when what they hold asks for more room than the row has (design 0011 §9), standing side by side again when it asks for less; panels in a grid stand in columns of set widths, one beside the several it spans (design 0012 §2: the Monitor's Flow beside Now, Watch for and the Guide), their borders lined up with the row below, and stack in the order given when the grid is narrow; a style's `round` border draws round corners; moving the focus costs the console no redraw of its own (design 0001 §5.7).

- a panel is never narrower than its title
- only the panel that holds the focus draws its border in border focused
- tab goes from panel to panel in reading order
- panels in a row share its height and stack when it is narrow
- a row whose content asks for more room stacks at once and stands side by side again after

### `tests/console/test_panels_everywhere.py`

Design 0011 §2, §4, §7, §8, §9 and §11 (R2): every section of every page is a panel, and nothing of a page sits outside one (the hint lines that ended some pages are the keys bar's now, design 0011 §7); every page of the console, the shell's, each component's and the planned ones, draws the panels it declares (`PANELS`), in that order, each a box with four borders and its title whole in its own top border, at 80, 120 and 170 columns, Requests' live view too (shown by `v`), each view of a page with views (the Monitor, design 0012 §2), which keeps its row of tabs and one heading line outside its panels (decision 0043), and each screen of a page shown one screen at a time (Add a model, design 0016 §14); with the machine column in a narrow window (F5 at 80 and 100 columns), a page narrower than one of its panels scrolls sideways to all of it rather than cut its title, in each of its views; a list in a panel is drawn on the panel's background, not on `surface` (a button's or a choice's), whether it has the focus or not; the phrases the console's tests look for are still found at 80 columns.

### `tests/console/test_providers_cloud.py`

Design 0016 §15: the Providers and models page's Cloud panel. With a provider: its title, its agreement, its key (its last four and its label, never the rest), its data policy, OpenRouter's own count for the key, Clank's spending today and this month against the limits, its last check and its presets, in the service's words; without one, how to add one. Each key calls its route: `a` Add a model at Cloud, `k` the key again in a hidden field, `c` the check (its items in a window), `o` the data policy (asked first), `L` the limits (a form, sent as typed), `r` remove (asked first; refused while in use, in the service's words).

- the cloud panel with a provider
- the cloud panel without a provider says how to add one
- k sets the key again in a hidden field
- c checks and shows each item
- o changes the data policy asked first
- capital l sets the limits in a form
- r removes asked first and while in use says why not

### `tests/console/test_questions.py`

Design 0002 §2.5 and design 0011 §12.1: the questions page lists every question waiting for the user, each with its detail, its options and who asked it when; the highlighted one is answered with an option's key, as you (console). Each question's options are buttons, clicked: a click on another question's option highlights it and answers it, the list keeping the focus.

- the questions page lists every waiting question and answers the highlighted one
- a click on another questions option highlights it and answers it the list keeping the focus

### `tests/console/test_records.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and design 0001 §5.4: the records page lists every kind of record (settings and state), the records of the chosen kind and the chosen record's values, each with its field's title. `?` explains what has the focus: a value (what it is, its value, where it comes from, the file that holds it or would, when a change applies, where its kind is defined), a planned menu entry (its milestone and design section), or the page itself. The command palette (Ctrl+P) finds every page and action by name and runs it.

- the records page lists the kinds then a kinds records then a records values
- question mark on a value explains it and where it is kept
- question mark on a planned menu entry names its milestone and design
- the command palette finds pages and actions by name and runs them

### `tests/console/test_requests_page.py`

Design 0001 §6 (the Requests page, 2026-10-15): F7 shows the model requests the gateway kept, newest first, a line each (when, who, status, tokens in and how much was cached, out, first token, total, speed), and the highlighted one whole: where it went, what was asked and what was answered. `v` shows the newest request live as the model writes it: what it does now, what was asked, and its text as it comes (asking only for what is new), following the next request once it ended. Design 0004 §2: a request the loop guard stopped is marked so. Design 0012 §4.1 (decision 0090): so is one whose engine refused it because the context was full, which OpenCode was told so that it compacts. Design 0011 §4 and §8 (board E16): how many requests are listed is the note in the top border of the list's panel; "No request kept yet." shows in that panel only while there is none; the live view is one panel, which `v` shows in place of the other two, and `v` again brings them back. Design 0016 §11: each request's cost ends its line, in the service's words, an estimate marked ≈; the highlighted one's details say the cost, how it was known, and its tokens in, cached, out and thinking. Design 0003 §4.1: the highlighted request says the sampling it sent of its own beside its preset's (the values the preset sets, else the engine's own defaults in words), none sent with the preset's in effect, or that it is not known when the gateway kept no copy of the request.

- f7 lists the requests newest first and shows the highlighted one whole
- with no request kept the lists panel says so and has no note
- one request kept is counted in the singular
- a request resent after its engine broke off says so
- a request the loop guard stopped says so
- a request refused because the context was full says so
- v follows the newest request live as the model writes it
- each requests cost shows in the services words an estimate marked and its details say how
- the sampling a request sent is shown beside its presets
- f7 shows the sampling a request sent beside its presets

### `tests/console/test_secret_field.py`

Design 0016 §4 and §14: the hidden field a key is typed or pasted into. It draws dots as the key comes, then `…` and its last four characters; the frame's text (and so a snapshot) and the console's log hold no other part of it; nothing copies it to the clipboard; Esc clears it, and taking it to send clears it too. A key's field in a form gives the key to the page that asked, and only once. Textual's own log (TEXTUAL_LOG) never holds it either: what is typed or pasted into the field never reaches a logger, and the worker that sends it is described, so its repr is not the call holding it; and `clank console` turns Textual's log file and devtools off.

- a key draws as dots then its last four
- a pasted key shows as dots and its last four and nothing else of it
- typed keys stay in the field and never reach the consoles own keys
- esc clears the field and gives nothing
- textuals own log holds no key typed pasted or sent
- clank console turns textuals log file and devtools off

### `tests/console/test_shell.py`

Design 0001 §5.1, §5.5, §5.7, design 0002 §2.15, §3.2 and design 0011 §7: the console's shell. Its menu lists every page it has, each with its key (the F-keys by their number, F10 after F9: design 0012 §2), then every page a later milestone brings, in red, each with its milestone; a planned entry opens a red page that says which milestone builds it and what it will do (the owner's request, 2026-10-11); a highlight overtaken by a newer one before it was heard never takes the menu back. The header shows the service's uptime, Docker's state and the questions waiting, or that the service is not running. The style sheet in CLANK_HOME is loaded after Clank's; animations are off; a burst of events is drawn in a few redraws, at most four a second, and nothing is lost. In the style's look (design 0011 §7): the header is one row on `bars`, the folder's name in `name` at normal weight on every page ("◆ CLANK" alone bold), each pill whole in its state's color and the clock in `quiet`; the menu's keys are in `key`, its highlighted entry on `selection` in `selected-text`, bold, whether the menu has the focus or not, and "coming" in `quiet`; the message line stands above the keys bar, in `quiet`, an error in `attention`, bold. Design 0016 §14 and decision 0105: with no model usable, a bare `clank` and `clank console` open on Add a model in place of This folder or Home, the header saying "no model: Add a model"; a page named on the command line opens as asked, the header still saying so; with a model ready, the console opens as it always did.

- the menu lists every page with its key then every planned page in red with its milestone
- the menu sorts its f keys by their number so f10 follows f9
- a planned entry opens a red page naming its milestone and what it will do
- a menu highlight overtaken by a newer one never takes the menu back
- the header shows the services uptime dockers state and the questions waiting
- the header says when the service is not running
- a style sheet in clank home is loaded after clanks
- animations are off
- home at 120 and at 80 columns
- a burst of events is drawn in a few redraws and nothing is lost
- the header is one row on bars naming the folder in name not bold on every page
- each pill is drawn whole in its states color and the clock in quiet
- the menus keys are in key and its highlighted entry on selection in selected text bold
- the message line stands above the keys bar in quiet and an error in attention bold
- with no model usable clank opens on add a model the header saying so
- with a model ready clank opens on this folder
- a page named on the command line opens as asked the header saying no model
- a bare clank and clank console open the guide and a page named does not

### `tests/console/test_terminal.py`

Design 0004 §1: handing the console's terminal to a program. The console steps aside, the program runs attached to the terminal, the keys it left unread are dropped, and the console comes back; a program that cannot start, or one interrupted, still gives the console back, saying so; headless (tests, snapshots) the program runs without stepping aside; a terminal resized meanwhile tells the console its new size; and Ctrl+C belongs to the program while it runs.

- the console steps aside runs the program drops its unread keys and comes back
- a program that cannot start or is interrupted still gives the console back
- headless the program runs without stepping aside
- a terminal resized while the program ran tells the console its size
- a program runs attached with ctrl c its own and the console s handler back after

### `tests/console/test_text.py`

Design 0002 §3.2: the console shows a state in color: green when it is as wanted, yellow while it changes, red when it is not; text is shown as it is, never read as markup; a state that appears more than once is colored each time, in order. Design 0016 §11: a cost is shown in US dollars, cents from a dollar up, two significant digits below, an estimate marked ≈; a model's window in thousands of tokens.

- a state is green when as wanted yellow while it changes and red when not
- each mark is colored where it next appears and the text is never read as markup
- money is cents from a dollar up and two significant digits below an estimate marked
- a window of tokens is said in thousands

### `tests/console/test_windows.py`

Design 0011 §4, §7, §11 and §12.1 (R2; every key a button): the windows over the console, the details window (`?`, `v`), the picker, the form, an MCP server's tools and a device's traffic, are each one panel (the traffic window two: its list and the highlighted exchange), their title in the top border, no longer a bold first line; a window's border is `border-focused`, since it has the focus (in the traffic window, the border of the panel that holds it), on the page's `background`; its keys ("Esc closes this") stay its last line, in `quiet`, the details window's a button, `esc close`, which has the focus when it opens. A window is never wider than the screen allows: a title too long for it is cut in the border with "…" and shown whole as the window's first line (design 0011 §4 and §5, revised after approval with decision 0081: nothing cut is lost).

- a window is one panel its title in its border its border focused its keys its last line
- the traffic window is two panels its list and the highlighted exchange
- a window title too long for the screen is cut in its border and shown whole inside

### `tests/console/test_workspaces_page.py`

Design 0004 §3, §10 (the Workspaces page, 2026-10-15): F6 lists every workspace with its container's state, its software and its folder; the highlighted one shows its container and limits, its use now (from the meters), its software and the MCP servers its agent gets; `x` stops its container. Design 0011 §4 and §8 (board E15): how many workspaces there are and run is the note in the top border of the list's panel, drawn again when it changes, and the highlighted workspace's name the note of its; "No workspace yet" shows in the list's panel only while there is none.

- f6 lists the workspaces and shows the highlighted one whole
- with no workspace the lists panel says so and has no note
- a workspaces detail says its use and which servers its agent gets

### `tests/contract/test_agent_program.py`

Design 0004 §4 and decision 0011: every agent program adapter meets the same contract. It says the command its program runs in a workspace's container and the environment it runs with (plain names), and writes, for a model reached at a URL, a configuration that names that URL, that model and its limits, kept in the container's home (never in the user's folder); and what it supports is declared.

- its command and environment are well formed
- its configuration names the model and lives in the containers home

### `tests/contract/test_container_platform.py`

Design 0002 §2.10 and design 0001 §8.2-8.3: every container platform adapter meets the same contract, run against what its program answered at each recorded version, as it ran where it was recorded (that platform, its command where it was installed): running, it reports its version, its engine's and its containers, with Clank's labels read exactly and every label's name; stopped, no containers, read within a few seconds although the stopped program leaves some questions unanswered; a start and a stop are asked of the program and change its state, even when the program's own start does nothing; without its command it is not installed and refuses to start; what it supports is read from the program.

- running it reports its version its engine and its containers
- clanks labels are read exactly and every labels name is listed
- stopped it reports no containers within a few seconds
- a start and a stop are asked of the program and change its state
- without its command it is not installed and refuses to start
- what it supports is read from the program

### `tests/contract/test_endpoint.py`

Design 0016 §18 (design 0015 §14): every endpoint adapter meets the same contract, run against every HTTP recording of its provider (`tests/fixtures/<adapter>/<date>/http.json`), the documented shapes first and the owner's recordings after: it reads the recorded list of models, the key's account, a whole answer's and a stream's cost, and each refusal into words, without an error; and the fake that stands in for the provider in every other test answers each recorded request as recorded.

- every endpoint adapter has a recording
- it reads the recorded list of models and the keys account
- it reads a whole answers and a streams cost from their usage
- it reads each recorded refusal into words
- the fake answers each recorded request as recorded

### `tests/contract/test_engine.py`

Design 0001 §8.2-8.3, design 0003 §4 and decision 0010: every engine adapter meets the same contract, run against what its engine printed at each recorded version: its options are well formed (each key once, each default valid, each choice listed), every flag they map to is in the engine's own `--help`, its startup log says when it is ready and what memory it took, and what its version supports is declared.

- its options are well formed
- every flag its options map to is in its recorded help
- its recorded startup says when it is ready and what memory it took
- what its version supports is declared

### `tests/contract/test_host.py`

Design 0003 §2 and decision 0030: every host meets the same contract: a process it starts is alive by its identity until it is stopped, stops once, and is gone after; it reads a file's facts; and it runs a step (of an install) to its end in its folder, with its environment, each line of its output given as it comes, and its exit status. A host whose program is the running system itself runs the contract live; any other runs it against what its program answered at each recorded version, with the inputs the recording was made with.

- a started process is alive by its identity until it stops once
- it reads a files facts
- a step runs to its end in its folder with its environment each line as it comes

### `tests/docker/test_escape.py`

Design 0004 §3 and §8, design 0002 §3.3 (M4's escape tests), on this machine's Docker when asked (`-m docker`): a workspace's container sees its folder and nothing else of the machine (its mounts, a subprocess, a symlink that points out of the folder); from inside it nothing answers but the edge: not the internet (by address or by name, IPv4 or IPv6), not the machine, not another workspace; the edge answers, forwarding to the gateway's port; and the container runs as the image's user, with no capability, within its limits.

- a container sees its folder and nothing else of the machine
- from inside nothing answers but the edge
- a container runs within its limits

### `tests/fakes/test_recorded.py`

Design 0001 §8.3: adapters are tested against what the real program answered at its pinned version. The recorded program answers each command as recorded for its current state, moves between states on the recorded commands, and fails on any command that was never recorded, so an adapter that starts asking something new is caught. It keeps time as the real program did: an answer that never came, or came later than the caller waits, is lost, the caller having waited its whole time limit; an application it launches answers by its name; and it says on which platform it was recorded and where its command was.

- the recorded program answers by state and moves on its transitions
- it can start in another state
- a command never recorded fails the test
- an answer that never came keeps the caller waiting its whole time limit
- an answer later than the time limit is lost and changes nothing
- an application it launches answers by its name and moves it on

### `tests/kernel/test_access.py`

Design 0002 §2.7: local clients prove they are the user on this machine by reading the local token, find the service through its connection file, and what they do is recorded as the user, through that client.

- the token is created once and then kept
- the token is long and random
- the token file is readable only by the user
- the connection file round trips and is removed on stop
- a clients name becomes the actor of what it does

### `tests/kernel/test_adapters.py`

Design 0002 §2.10: each kind of adapter has an interface and the list of its adapters; an adapter that does not implement the whole interface, has no name, label or platform, or reuses a name, is refused.

- a kind lists its adapters and finds them by name and platform
- an unknown adapter is refused naming the known ones
- an adapter missing a method of the interface is refused
- an adapter without a name or with a name used twice is refused
- an adapter of another kind is refused

### `tests/kernel/test_api.py`

Design 0002 §2.7 and §2.14: every route of the Clank API needs the local token and answers only to the service's own host names; errors have one shape; every kind of record is read and written through the same routes; questions are answered as the client that sent the answer; the event stream sends what a client missed, then what happens, without gaps or repeats, and tells a client behind the pruned history to reload.

- the api describes every route
- every route refuses a call without the token
- every route declares its access level
- a request addressed to another host is refused
- errors have one shape
- the service says what it is
- kinds are listed with their labels help and schemas
- a settings record is written over its revision and refused over a stale one
- state records are read through the same routes and never written through them
- kinds say when a change to their records applies and whose start applies it
- kinds say where they are defined and a settings record where the users file is
- answering a question records the client that answered
- an operations log is read through the api from any line on
- the stream sends what a client missed in order
- the stream tells a client behind the pruned history to reload
- the stream sends live events from other threads without gaps or repeats
- a page size outside one to a thousand is refused in the one error shape

### `tests/kernel/test_clock.py`

Design 0002 §2.3: times are kept in UTC as text that sorts in time order, and tests move a fake clock by hand.

- the fake clock moves only when told
- times round trip through text in utc
- times as text sort in time order
- a time without a zone is refused

### `tests/kernel/test_console_kinds.py`

Design 0011 §2 (the style: a file of named elements) and §3 (the console's setting that picks it): Clank's own style holds the boards' values; an owner's style that names a few elements keeps every other element of Clank's; a color that is not #rrggbb, a glyph two cells wide, and a bar's eighths that are not eight glyphs, are refused, naming the field; the console's setting picks `clank` until it is changed.

- clanks own style holds the boards values
- an owners style naming a few elements keeps every other of clanks
- a color that is not rrggbb is refused naming its field
- a glyph two cells wide is refused naming its field
- a bars eighths that are not eight glyphs are refused naming their field
- the consoles setting picks clank until it is changed

### `tests/kernel/test_core.py`

Design 0002 §2.1 and decision 0013: the kernel is assembled from its own parts and every component's declarations; each component's part is built with the kernel, and its operation types join the kernel's.

- opening a kernel creates the database loads the settings and builds every part
- a components operation types join the kernels

### `tests/kernel/test_event_attention.py`

Design 0002 §2.3 (decision 0113): an event type declares whether its events need the owner's attention, and each event carries it, as recorded, as heard, as published live and as read back; the events page says it; questions asked and the cloud's spending limits and refusals are the first that need the owner (design 0016 §11, §17).

- an event carries its types attention as recorded heard and read back
- the events page says which types need the owner
- questions asked and the clouds limits and refusals need the owner

### `tests/kernel/test_events.py`

Design 0002 §2.3: every event is kept with an id that only grows, is heard by listeners only once it is committed, can be followed from any point, and tells a client that is too far behind that it missed some.

- ids only grow and an event reads back as written
- an undeclared type is refused
- a live only event reaches listeners but is never kept
- following from an id returns later events filtered by type and subject
- listeners hear an event only after its transaction commits
- a listener that fails does not stop the others
- an unsubscribed listener hears nothing more
- the latest events come newest first a page at a time
- a client behind the pruned events is told it missed some

### `tests/kernel/test_grouped_settings.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and design 0013 §11 (decision 0066): a settings kind may keep its records one folder deeper, grouped (a flow is `config/flows/<workspace>/<name>.json`): each record is named by its group and its name, read, written over its revision and recorded like any other; a file that belongs to no group is reported; an id never reaches outside the kind's folder; and the record routes read and write a grouped record by its id.

- a grouped kinds records are read from a folder per group each named by its group and its name
- a file directly in a grouped kinds folder is reported saying where it belongs
- a grouped record is written into its groups folder over its revision and refused over another
- an id that names no group or climbs out of the kinds folder is refused
- the record routes read and write a grouped record by its id

### `tests/kernel/test_home.py`

Design 0001 §7.2 and design 0002 §2.16: everything of one user lives under CLANK_HOME (`~/.clank` unless the environment names another folder), in the folders the kernel creates.

- clank home from the environment wins
- the default is dot clank in the home folder
- ensure creates every folder
- a homes id is the same every time and differs between homes

### `tests/kernel/test_live_stream.py`

Design 0002 §2.3 and §2.14: over real HTTP, the event stream first says it is connected, then every client that follows it hears who is connected, as clients join and leave (a live event, not kept).

- the stream says it is connected then who is connected

### `tests/kernel/test_operations.py`

Design 0002 §2.4: anything slow runs in the service as an operation: its steps recorded as they start and end, locks that refuse a second one, request keys that never start one twice, cancellation, waiting for the user's answer, and after a restart, operations marked interrupted and reconciled instead of blindly repeated.

- an operation runs its steps in order and records each
- an operations log is kept with the step of each line and read from any line on
- a failing step fails the operation and stops it
- a second operation needing the same lock is refused naming the first
- the same request key returns the operation already started
- canceling stops a cancelable operation at its next check
- an operation that cancels itself with a reason shows that reason
- an operation that cannot be canceled says so
- waiting for the user resumes with the answer
- an answer given from another thread wakes the operation
- an expired question resumes it with the default
- expiry without an expiry time leaves the question waiting
- after a restart unfinished operations are interrupted their questions withdrawn and reconciled
- retrying an interrupted operation runs it again when its steps are repeatable
- retrying is refused when a step that had started is not repeatable

### `tests/kernel/test_processes.py`

Design 0002 §2.9: Clank runs a program to read its answer with a time limit; a program that is missing, fails or takes too long gives no answer instead of an error, within its time limit even when a process it started holds its output open, and output in UTF-16 (as `wsl.exe` writes it) is decoded. A long command (a build) is followed line by line as it writes, to its exit status; canceled, it is ended with every process it started. An application (Docker Desktop) is started to run on its own, without waiting for it and without a window.

- a programs output is returned
- a programs whole answer keeps its exit status its output and its errors
- a failing missing or slow program gives no answer
- a slow program gives no answer within its time limit even when a process it started holds its output
- an application is started to run on its own without waiting for it and without a window
- output in utf16 is decoded
- a streamed commands lines come as written then its exit status
- a canceled command is ended with the processes it started

### `tests/kernel/test_question_numbers.py`

Design 0002 §2.5 (decision 0114): a question's option may ask for a number, with its unit, its bounds and its step (`{"kind": "number", "unit", "min", "max", "step"}`, the shape every client reads), so every client answers it the same way; an answer of that option takes a number within the bounds, on the step, and one without a number, with a word, out of bounds or off the step is refused before anyone hears of it, saying the bounds and the unit; the question keeps the declaration, and the answer the number.

- an option that asks a number takes one within its bounds
- an answer without a number in bounds is refused saying them
- a number asked needs bounds and a step that make sense

### `tests/kernel/test_questions.py`

Design 0002 §2.5: a question is answered once, from any client, with who answered, through which client and when; it takes its default when its time is up, can be withdrawn, survives a restart, and each step is an event.

- asking records the question and announces it
- an answer records who gave it through which client and when
- a second answer is refused naming the first
- an answer must be one of the options
- a text question needs words or one of its suggestions
- a draft without enough options or with a default that is not an option is refused
- a question whose time is up takes its default and says so
- a withdrawn question cannot be answered
- questions survive reopening the database
- listeners hear an answer after it is recorded

### `tests/kernel/test_records.py`

Design 0002 §2.2: every kind of record is one Pydantic class, declared once, whose every field has a label and help for the explain key.

- a kind declared twice is refused
- a kind missing its constants is refused
- a field without a label or help is found in nested models too
- settings kinds name their folder
- every kind clank declares has every field documented

### `tests/kernel/test_secrets.py`

Design 0002 §2.8: a secret is a file of its own in `CLANK_HOME/secrets/`, readable only by the user; clients see its name, when it was set and its last four characters, never its value; a component gets its value through the kernel, which records who used it and why, without the value; setting and removing one are recorded the same way.

- a secret is a file of its own that only the user can read
- clients see its name when it was set and its last four characters
- using setting and removing one are recorded without its value
- a name that could leave its folder is refused

### `tests/kernel/test_service.py`

Design 0002 §1 and §2.7, decision 0013: `clank serve` runs the Clank service: it takes its port (refusing clearly when another program has it, or when a service already runs for the same CLANK_HOME), writes its connection file, answers only with the token, keeps house (expiring questions, reloading edited settings), and stops on request, removing the file; what a stop cut short is interrupted at the next start.

- the service answers only with the token and stops on request
- a stop ends the event stream at once telling its followers and logs no error
- a second service for the same clank home is refused
- a service still starting for the same home is refused before it writes its connection file
- a port taken by another program is refused with a clear message
- the housekeeping expires questions and reloads an edited settings file
- an operation cut short by a stop is interrupted at the next start

### `tests/kernel/test_settings.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and decision 0007: settings are readable JSON files that name their schema, replace Clank's own defaults by name, are written in one fixed format only over the version they were read from, report any problem with its file, line and field while the last good values stay in force, and record every change, told to their listeners once no lock is held: a listener may wait for another thread that reads the settings (the event stream waits for the service's event loop, where the flows' driver reads them).

- a single record kind without a file has its built in values
- a user file replaces clanks default of the same name
- files are written in one fixed format
- a byte order mark is accepted
- invalid json is reported with its line and the last good values stay
- a wrong type and an unknown field are reported with file line and field
- a file without its own current schema is reported
- an older file is migrated and its original kept
- a write based on an old revision is refused with the current version
- a change to a record in use is refused when its kind says so
- a write records each fields old and new value
- a file edited on disk is reloaded and recorded as changed by a person
- deleting a user file brings clanks default back
- a problem in a nested or repeated field names that fields own line
- a change is told with no lock held so a listener may wait for a reader

### `tests/kernel/test_settings_checks.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and design 0013 §14 (its step 1: an unknown preset is refused): a component may check a kind's records against other records, which the kind's own validation cannot see (a flow's presets); a record that check finds wrong is refused when written, naming each field, and its file is reported when read, with its line and field, the last good values staying in force; and a check added after the files were read checks the records read before.

- a record the components check finds wrong is refused when written naming each field
- a file the components check finds wrong is reported with its line and field and the last good values stay
- a check added after the files were read checks the records read before

### `tests/kernel/test_store.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and decision 0014: state lives in one SQLite database, written in transactions that commit or roll back whole, with numbered migrations that are applied once and never edited.

- migrations apply once and in order
- an applied migration that was edited is refused
- a failing migration leaves nothing behind
- a transaction that fails writes nothing
- nested transactions commit or roll back together
- callbacks run only after the outermost commit and never after a rollback
- a callback needs an open transaction
- the database uses write ahead logging
- the kernels migrations create its tables

### `tests/kernel/test_where.py`

Design 0013 §15 (decision 0099): where a planning opens is where the owner is, as the console's or the command's own environment says: over SSH (`SSH_CONNECTION`, `SSH_CLIENT` or `SSH_TTY` set), always in that terminal, whatever asked for VS Code, said so, since VS Code would open on the PC's own screen; else what was asked; else in VS Code when the console runs in VS Code's terminal (`TERM_PROGRAM`), else here. From the console (decision 0100), a planning never takes the console's terminal: over SSH nothing opens (a message says how to attach), in Windows Terminal its window is split with `wt -w 0 split-pane -V -d <folder> <python> -m clank flow attach --workspace <W>` (`--home` when `CLANK_HOME` is set, `;` escaped), in VS Code's terminal VS Code, elsewhere the message; a split that cannot run says why.

- any of ssh s variables says the terminal is over ssh
- over ssh it opens in this terminal whatever asked for vs code and says why
- elsewhere it opens where asked else in vs code when the console runs there else here
- from the console over ssh nothing opens whatever else is set
- from the console windows terminal is split vs codes terminal opens vs code elsewhere the message
- windows terminals split is its documented command line with clanks home and semicolons escaped
- a split that cannot run says why

### `tests/real/test_engines.py`

Design 0003 §9 and the M1.6 plan's task 2: on this machine, when asked (`-m real`), each shipped preset whose engine is installed here starts through a load, answers a request for its model, is measured (what it took, and what it needed to start when its engine says), and stops through an unload, its GPU memory freed.

- each preset installed here starts answers is measured and stops

### `tests/real/test_openrouter.py`

Design 0016 §18: the checks owed with the owner's key, against OpenRouter itself. Never in CI: run only when asked (`uv run pytest -m openrouter`), with the key typed for the run in `CLANK_TEST_OPENROUTER_KEY` (never stored), on Clank's made-up words only, to the cheapest model that takes tool calls (`CLANK_TEST_OPENROUTER_MODEL` names another). Each answer that differs from the documentation revises design 0016 §3 and the adapter in one commit.

- check 1 the models list with and without the key
- check 2 and 7 a streams id is its generation and its last message has the cost
- check 7 a whole answer has its cost
- check 10 the keys own facts read
- check 6 and 5 a refused key reads into words

### `tests/test_architecture.py`

Decision 0019 and design 0002 §2.1: the kernel uses no component; a component uses another only through that one's `service.py`, only if its declaration names it, and never in a cycle; every component has the same shape; and the service never loads the console's library (a component's pages are imported only by the console). Every kind of adapter has its contract tests and every adapter its recordings (design 0002 §2.10): an endpoint's are of HTTP (design 0016 §18).

- the kernel uses no component and no client
- a component uses another only through its service and only if it declares it
- the uses between components have no cycle
- every component has the same shape and is listed
- every kind of adapter has its contract tests and every adapter its recordings
- the endpoint kind of adapter is the runtimes
- the service never loads the console

### `tests/test_repository.py`

Design 0001 §8.4, design 0002 §3.2 and design 0011 §6: the repository passes its own checks, so `pytest` alone catches what continuous integration would: a generated document out of date, a broken link, a secret, a British spelling, a color or a state's mark written in the console's code.

- the code map is up to date
- the tests page is up to date
- the events page is up to date
- the api page is up to date
- the extensions typescript types are up to date
- the settings schemas are up to date
- clanks own default settings read without a problem
- every link index and section status is in order
- nothing looks secret or personal
- everything is written in american english
- the console names only the styles colors and marks

### `tests/test_version.py`

Design 0002 §3.2 (M0.1): `clank --version` reports the version of the code that is running.

- clank version prints the version of the code
- the package metadata carries the same version
- python dash m clank runs the command from any folder
- clank help shows how to use it and that clank alone opens the console on this folder

### `tests/tools/test_apidoc.py`

Design 0002 §2.14: the API page lists every route the service serves, with its method, its access level and what it does.

- the api page lists every route with its access and purpose

### `tests/tools/test_capture_endpoint.py`

Design 0016 §18: the capture of OpenRouter's answers to Clank's fixed made-up requests, run here against the fake OpenRouter (never the network): it records each exchange the contract reads, never the key, and replaces what the account says of the owner (its label, limit, use and ids) by made-up values; without the key's variable it records nothing.

- the capture records each exchange never the key and the account made up
- without the keys variable nothing is recorded

### `tests/tools/test_check.py`

Design 0001 §8.4: one command runs every check that continuous integration runs, names each one that fails, and with `--fix` first rewrites what can be rewritten safely.

- every check runs and success returns zero
- a failure does not stop the other checks and is named
- fix rewrites first then checks
- the types are checked as on windows and as on linux each with its own cache

### `tests/tools/test_check_colors.py`

Design 0011 §6: the colors check refuses a color or a state's mark written in the console's code, where only the style's elements may be named: a hex color, an `rgb()` or `hsl()` value, a color's name in a style rule (a `.tcss` file or a widget's `DEFAULT_CSS`), a style string or markup that names a color, a `$` variable that is not the style's, and the marks ● ○ ✓ ✗ ◆ ✎ in a string; each finding names its file and line. Prose passes: a docstring, or a sentence shown to the owner, may name a color; ?, · and … are punctuation too. The repository itself passes (`tests/test_repository.py`).

- a hex color in a rule or a style string is refused
- an rgb or hsl value is refused
- a colors name in a rule is refused
- a colors name in a widgets default css is refused
- a style string or markup that names a color is refused
- a variable that is not the styles is refused
- a states mark written in a page is refused naming the style mark to draw
- prose and punctuation and the styles own names pass

### `tests/tools/test_check_docs.py`

Design 0001 §8.4: the documentation check finds links to files or headings that do not exist, documents missing from their index, and design sections without a status, reading the documents the way GitHub shows them.

- a link to a missing file is reported
- a link to a missing heading is reported and a real one is not
- anchors follow github for punctuation section signs code and repeats
- links inside code are not checked
- outside links are not checked
- a link out of the repository is reported
- documents missing from their index and gaps in numbers are reported
- design sections missing from the table or without a status are reported
- tidy documents have no problems
- setext headings have anchors as github gives them
- a link from the repositorys root resolves there
- an index linking with a leading dot lists the document

### `tests/tools/test_check_secrets.py`

Design 0001 §8.4: the secrets check finds keys, tokens, home folders and machine identifiers in any text file, names the file, line and kind but never the value, skips files that are not text, and leaves placeholders alone.

- each kind of secret is found and named
- a finding names the file and line but not the value
- placeholders and home shorthand are not findings
- the names of the user and the computer are found unless generic
- the users of containers and services are generic names
- the repositorys own home on github is not personal
- files that are not text are skipped
- the check does not trip on its own patterns
- environment placeholders and generic users homes are not findings

### `tests/tools/test_check_spelling.py`

Design 0002 §3.2 (M0.1): the spelling check finds British forms in code and documents and names the American one; a line marked `spelling: keep`, a Markdown code block, and a file that is not text are left alone.

- a british form is found with its american form
- capitals and identifiers do not hide a british form
- american forms and longer words are not findings
- a line marked keep is left alone
- markdown code blocks are skipped but code files are not
- files that are not text are skipped

### `tests/tools/test_codemap.py`

Design 0002 §3.2 (M0.1): the code map lists every module of `clank/` and `tools/` with the first line of its docstring, and refuses a module that has none.

- each module gets one line from its docstring
- paths use forward slashes and a stable order
- every module without a docstring is named in one error
- a module that is not valid python is reported not crashed on
- a pipe in a docstring does not break the table
- the extensions typescript modules get their line from their leading comment
- a typescript module without a leading comment is named
- a module git ignores is not in the map

### `tests/tools/test_eventsdoc.py`

Design 0002 §2.3: the events page lists every declared event type, grouped by the part that declares it, with what it means, its data, and whether it is kept.

- the page lists every type with its meaning and data

### `tests/tools/test_extension.py`

Design 0001 §8.4 and decision 0028: `tools.check` checks the VS Code extension too: it installs the extension's tools from their lock when they are missing or older than the lock, then compiles and tests it; without npm it says what to install.

- without npm it says what to install
- it installs from the lock when the tools are missing then checks
- with its tools installed it only checks
- asked for the host suite it runs it inside vscode after the checks
- without a display the host suite runs under a virtual one

### `tests/tools/test_files.py`

Design 0001 §8.4: the checks see exactly the files a commit would hold (tracked, or new and not ignored), and read as text every file that is text.

- tracked and new files are seen and ignored ones are not
- a file with a nul byte is not text
- text that is not utf8 is still read
- a folder outside git is listed whole but for caches

### `tests/tools/test_generated.py`

Design 0002 §3.2 (M0.1): a generated document is written with LF line endings on every platform, and `--check` reports, without writing anything, whether the copy in the repository is up to date.

- writing uses lf line endings on every platform
- check passes when the copy matches
- check fails names the command and writes nothing when the copy differs
- check fails when the document is missing

### `tests/tools/test_markdown.py`

Design 0001 §8.4 and design 0001 §8.5: documents are read as GitHub reads them, so a heading or a link inside a code block is code, not part of the document.

- lines inside a fence are code and line numbers are kept
- a longer fence holds a shorter one
- tildes close only a tilde fence
- a fence line with text after it does not close a fence
- a fence indented inside a list item is code

### `tests/tools/test_schemas.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and decision 0015: every settings kind's files have a JSON Schema generated from the kind's one definition, carrying its fields' labels and help and the "schema" constant every file starts with.

- a settings kinds schema carries its fields their labels and help and its schema constant

### `tests/tools/test_testspage.py`

Design 0001 §8.5: the tests page lists each design section with the test files that name it, says which sections have no tests yet, and refuses a test file that names no section, or a section that no design has.

- a section lists the test files that name it
- sections without tests say so and only leaves are counted
- a test file is listed under each section of a list and under a section named across a line break
- a section of a list that does not exist is refused
- headings inside code blocks are not sections
- a test file that names no section is refused
- a section that does not exist is refused
- tests in classes and asynchronous tests are listed
- the extensions typescript test files are placed by their leading comment
- a typescript test file that names no section is refused
- a test file git ignores is not on the page

### `tests/tools/test_tstypes.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 (decisions 0015, 0028): the TypeScript types of Clank's kinds are generated from their JSON Schemas, as the API sends records (whole: every field present, defaulted ones too): strings, numbers, booleans, nullable values, literal choices, arrays, maps, nested objects and references to definitions, each field with its description.

- simple values map to their typescript
- choices nullable values and references map to unions and names
- arrays maps and nested objects
- each kind and each definition it uses becomes one interface with its descriptions
- a kind named with a hyphen is a quoted key of the records by kind

### `tests/tools/test_vsix.py`

Design 0002 §3.2 (decision 0028): the VS Code extension is packaged as a `.vsix` by Clank's own tool: the manifest naming the extension, its version, its publisher and the VS Code it needs, the content types, and the extension's manifest, its README and its compiled files; nothing of its tests or its tools; and nothing at all before it is compiled.

- the package holds the manifests the compiled extension and its media only
- the manifest names the extension its version and the vs code it needs
- a package is refused before the extension is compiled

### `tests/vscode/test_client.py`

Design 0002 §2.5 and §2.7: the VS Code extension's client, run by Node against a real Clank service, lists the question waiting and answers it; the service records the answer as you (vscode), as it would one from the console.

- the extensions client answers a question recorded as you through vscode

### `vscode/test/agent.test.ts`

Design 0004 §5: in Clank's VS Code window the agent starts beside the files: for the folder the window has open, the folder's workspace is read; when its container runs, the agent's configuration is written into it and the terminal runs the command the service answers, named after the program and the workspace; otherwise it says what is missing and how to get it ready, and writes nothing. A window that has the agent's terminal (running, or only its tab, restored with a reloaded window before VS Code revives it) does not start another. With one OpenCode window per workspace (the service closed the others first), the new terminal replaces the window's agent terminals. The agent's group stays at the right edge: moved there when it is not, the keyboard given back to the group that had it.

- a folder whose container runs gets the agent's terminal, with the command the service answers
- with one window per workspace the new terminal replaces the window's agent terminals, and otherwise adds one
- a folder that is not ready says what is missing and how to get it ready, and writes nothing
- the agent's terminal counts as open when it runs, or when only its tab came back with a reloaded window
- the agent's group is moved to the right edge, and the keyboard goes back to the group that had it

### `vscode/test/annotator.page.test.ts`

Design 0008 §6, in a real browser: the annotator page (media/annotator.html, as VS Code's webview shows it) in headless Edge, driven by mouse and keyboard events through the browser's debugging protocol. The box tool is chosen on the toolbar and a box dragged on the figure: the mark is kept in the image's own pixels; a message typed and Enter pressed sends the marks, the message and the marked-up copy (a PNG); a reply that it went clears the marks and says where it went. A note written near the figure's corner opens as a bubble that stays on the figure, its words wrapped; dragged, only its bubble moves (its pin stays where it was clicked); a click on it opens it to edit. Skipped where no Edge is installed (the Linux runs); screenshots of the page are left in out/test/ to look at.

- a box drawn on the figure and a message sent leave the page with the marks, the words and the marked-up copy
- a note opens as a bubble that stays on the figure; dragged, only the bubble moves; a click edits it

### `vscode/test/client.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.7 and §2.14: the extension's client sends the local token and names itself `vscode` on every call, a request that starts an operation carries its request key, the API's error shape becomes a `ClankError` with the service's own words, and a service that does not answer is `NotRunning`.

- every call carries the token and names the client vscode
- a request that starts an operation carries its request key and its body
- the API's error shape becomes a ClankError with the service's own words
- a service that does not answer is NotRunning

### `vscode/test/completion.test.ts`

Design 0006 §5: completion asks the gateway only while it is on and its slot serves, after the wait, not when it was canceled meanwhile nor when the rest of the line holds more than closing brackets; it sends the lines before the current one (up to 120), the current line to the cursor and the lines after (up to 20); one line, or a few on an empty line after one that opens a block; the suggestion is one line when one was asked. The status bar item says whether it is on and served, and a click turns it off or on.

- a request carries the lines before, the line to the cursor and the lines after; few lines after a block opens
- it asks only when the rest of the line is closing brackets or quotes
- a suggestion is one line when one was asked, without trailing spaces
- it asks the gateway after the wait while it is on and served, and not when canceled, off or not served
- the status bar says whether completion is on and served, and a click turns it off and on

### `vscode/test/connection.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.7: the extension finds the service through CLANK_HOME (its setting, else the environment, else `.clank` in the home folder), reading the connection file and the local token each time; a missing or unreadable file means the service is not running.

- the setting wins, then the environment, then .clank in the home folder
- the connection file and the token are read when both are there

### `vscode/test/controller.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.5, §2.7 and §3.2: the extension's controller keeps the status bar current (the service and the questions waiting, Docker's state), says when the service is not running and connects once one answers, shows a question asked, or one already waiting when VS Code opens, as a notification (each once) and answers it with the option chosen, says who answered when the question was answered elsewhere first, answers a question picked from the list, starts and stops Docker with a request key, and sends presence while the user types, at most once a minute. Design 0003 §8.3: the status bar shows what the main slot runs (or its state and why, in the tooltip), a slot's events keep it current, and Switch model picks a preset and switches the main slot with a request key; clicking the Clank item answers the questions when some wait, else switches the model. Design 0013 §7: when a flow's box switched the main slot, the window is told its model changed, once, as a notification in the service's words. Design 0016 §17: with a cloud model in the main slot, the Clank item says "cloud" and its tooltip today's and this month's spending in the service's words, and the pause; Switch model lists a cloud preset as its provider and model, with its price as the service says it.

- the status bar shows the service and the questions waiting, and Docker's state
- with no service it says not running, and connects once one answers
- a question asked shows as a notification, and the option chosen answers it
- questions already waiting when VS Code opens are each shown once, not again on reconnecting
- an option chosen after the question was answered elsewhere says who answered
- the questions command lists them and answers the one picked
- Docker is started and stopped with a request key
- typing sends presence at most once a minute
- the status bar shows what the main slot runs, or the slot's state and why in its tooltip
- Switch model picks a preset and switches the main slot with a request key
- clicking the Clank item answers the questions when some wait, else switches the model
- a window told its model changed shows it once, in the service's words
- a cloud model's item says cloud, its tooltip the spending in the service's words, and the pause
- Switch model lists a cloud preset as its provider and model, with its price

### `vscode/test/events.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.3 (decision 0017): the event stream's text is read into whole messages, whether a message arrives split across pieces or several arrive in one piece; a comment (the keepalive) is skipped, and a kept event has its id.

- a message split across pieces is read once it is whole
- several messages in one piece are each read, and comments are skipped

### `vscode/test/gates.test.ts`

Design 0013 §10 and §14 step 7 (design 0007 §6): a question about a flow shows in VS Code as a gate, a notification with its answers and, for the files it shows, Open and Diff. The answer chosen is sent; one that takes words (send back's note, try again's) asks for them first, and Escape keeps the gate waiting and shows it again. Open opens the file in the folder, and the gate comes back once that file is saved (edit, then approve); Diff opens the last two tries' copies in VS Code's diff, and the gate comes back. A gate with one try offers no Diff; a question about a flow that no run names shows with its options alone. The controller gives every question about a flow to the gates, the Questions command's too, and never shows it as a plain notification; the Questions command (the status bar's count) shows a gate again even while its first notification waits unclicked in the notification center, and the gate is answered once, from whichever of them is clicked first. Design 0002 §2.5: a gate answered on another client is never shown again here, its diffs close, a save of its file brings nothing back, and a click on its notification still on screen (VS Code cannot close one), or a note written meanwhile, sends nothing and says who answered it; one answered while VS Code was not connected is closed when it connects again.

- a question about a flow shows as a gate with its answers, Open and Diff
- the answer chosen is sent
- send back asks for the note and sends it, and Escape keeps the gate waiting and shows it again
- Open opens the file in the folder, and the gate comes back once it is saved
- a file a YOU box asks for, not written yet, opens as a new file at its place
- Diff opens the last two tries' copies in VS Code's diff, and the gate comes back
- a gate whose file has one try offers no Diff
- a gate showing several files picks the one to open and the one to diff
- a question about a flow that no run names shows with its options alone
- the controller gives a question about a flow to the gates, never as a plain notification
- the status bar's count shows a gate again while its first notification still waits, unclicked
- a gate shown again while its first notification waits is answered once, from either
- Diff brings the gate back even while an older notification of it still waits
- a gate answered on the phone is never shown again here, and its notification says who answered it
- a note written while the gate was answered elsewhere sends nothing
- a gate answered while VS Code was not connected is closed when it connects again

### `vscode/test/kinds.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.2 (decisions 0015, 0028): the TypeScript types generated from Clank's kinds describe what the API sends, so a question read from the API fits `Question`, field for field (the compiler checks it; the test reads it).

- a question as the API sends it fits the generated type

### `vscode/test/live.test.ts`

Design 0002 §2.5 and §2.7: against a real Clank service (the Python suite starts one and names it in CLANK_TEST_URL and CLANK_TEST_TOKEN), the client lists the question waiting and answers it with CLANK_TEST_OPTION.

- the client lists the waiting question and answers it

### `vscode/test/marks.test.ts`

Design 0008 §6: the annotator's marks are numbered as they are drawn, kept in the image's pixels, undone and redone (a clear too), dropped when too small to be meant; each is drawn at any scale with its number in a badge; a note is a bubble tied to its pin, its words wrapped inside it, kept on the figure wherever it is put, moved without moving its pin, edited (emptied, it goes and the marks after it are numbered again) and found by a click; and a send says where it went, with or without the marked-up figure.

- marks are numbered as they are drawn, and undo, redo and clear step through them
- a mark too small to be meant is dropped, and an arrow's head is at its tip
- a mark is drawn at the scale shown, with its number in a badge
- a note's words wrap inside its bubble, which stays on the figure, beside its pin
- a note's bubble moves without its pin, is kept on the figure, and the move is undone
- a note edited takes the new words; emptied, it goes and the marks after it are numbered again
- a click on a note's bubble finds the note; a click beside it does not
- a note is drawn as a bubble tied to its pin, a line for each of its lines
- a send says where it went, with or without the marked-up figure

### `vscode/test/notion.page.test.ts`

Design 0010 §6, in a real browser: the review page (media/notion.html, as VS Code's webview shows it) in headless Edge. A batch sent to it shows its database as a table: the changed row with what a cell was struck above what it would be, the removed and the added rows marked, a neighbor left as it is; the first change is chosen. Everything not written is checked at first; unchecking a row leaves it out of the approval, and unchecking a field of a change sends only the rest of it; a new entry's title cannot be left out while other parts of it go (unchecking it unchecks them, checking a part checks it). The page view shows the page block by block, the unchanged ones folded; As in Notion shows the entry as it will look. Shown again after an approval, the page keeps what the owner checked and marks what was written. A batch written whole cannot be approved again. Comments (§7): each thread shows under what it is on (the batch, a property, a block of the page), with the agent's answer; a comment is written there and sent to the extension, one not sent yet is taken back, and the batch is sent back with them; a proposal revised for them says what changed since the last revision. Screenshots of the page are left in out/test/.

- the review page shows the table, sends what is checked, and keeps the choices when shown again
- comments show under what they are on, are written and taken back there, and the batch is sent back

### `vscode/test/notion.test.ts`

Design 0010 §6: the Notion changes view lists each batch waiting with its databases and their entries (marked new, changed or removed, a result said beside each) and the batches written or rejected under "Earlier"; the status bar and the view's badge count the proposals waiting; a batch submitted, written or rejected reads the batches again, and the review open shows the batch as it is now. The review opens on a database or an entry, with the tables of changes and their neighbors, or every row when asked. Approving all asks first; approving what is checked sends the parts; nothing checked sends nothing. A rejection sends the reason; Escape keeps the batch waiting. A service without Notion shows nothing. Comments (§7): the owner comments on a property, takes a comment back and sends the batch back (the view keeps it, sent back, without counting it); approving while comments are unanswered asks first. Through the controller: the batches are read on connecting and followed with the events, and the view and the page act on them.

- the view lists each batch waiting, its databases and their entries, then the earlier ones
- the words of counts, times, the status bar and an approval
- loading counts the proposals waiting and a batch submitted reads them again
- the review opens on an entry, shows every row when asked, and follows the batch while open
- approving all asks first and approving the checked parts sends them
- a rejection sends the reason, and Escape keeps the batch waiting
- a service without Notion shows nothing
- through the controller, the batches are read on connecting, followed with the events, and acted on
- the owner comments, takes a comment back and sends the batch back, which stays listed but not counted
- approving while comments are unanswered asks first

### `vscode/test/plans.test.ts`

Design 0009 §7, §8: a Markdown file whose header says `kind: plan` (the same cases as Clank's own `is_plan` and `plan_path`) gets Implement with Review and Implement with Auto, and Continue when some of its checklist is done; the requests that hand the plan to the agent name the workspace, the plan's path in its folder with `/`, and the mode; a plan that is not saved is saved first; no OpenCode in a window offers to open the agent and tries once more.

- a header that says kind: plan makes a plan, and anything else is not one
- no file is a plan outside the folder, in a settings folder, or among the files that instruct agents
- the checklist counts - [x] and - [X] as done and - [ ] as not, and Continue needs some done
- the plan's path is relative to the workspace folder with / between parts
- implementing saves the plan, finds the workspace of its folder, and posts the plan's path and the mode
- a folder without a workspace, or a plan outside the folder, is said and nothing is posted
- with no OpenCode in a window, the agent is offered, opened, and the plan handed over once its window runs
- a window that never comes is waited for half a minute, then the plan is offered once more and the refusal said
- a look at the servers that fails is one more look, not the end of the wait
- the retry happens once, a declined offer stops, and other refusals are said as they come

### `vscode/test/review.test.ts`

Design 0007 §6: a change the agent asks for, in the window's folder, opens as a diff beside the files instead of a notification (one in another folder does not), and closes when its question is settled anywhere; Accept and Reject answer its question (a reject with the words given, none when Escape is pressed); a change settled while it is being opened never opens, and a tab left from before whose question no longer waits closes on loading; in auto mode a change made opens after it is made, unless the setting says not to; the status bar shows the mode and a click switches it.

- a change waiting in this window's folder opens beside the files, and closes when it is settled
- a change settled while it is being opened never opens: a quick answer leaves no tab
- on loading, a change's tab left open whose question no longer waits closes
- accept answers the change's question; reject says why, and Escape keeps it waiting
- in auto mode a change made in this folder opens after it is made, unless the setting says not to
- a click on the mode offers Plan, Review and Auto with the current one marked, and the pick is posted
- a pick dismissed posts nothing, and the status bar shows Plan only when the service says shown is plan
- the status bar follows the mode, agent and review events of the service
- the mode's tooltip says what waits and why the agent is not followed
- the controller gives a question about a change to the review, not to a notification

### `vscode/test/state.test.ts`

Design 0002 §1 and §2.3: what the extension shows is read from the API and kept current by events: a question's events change the questions, Docker's change Docker, the service's change the service, a slot's change the slots, and a preset's settings change the presets.

- each event names the topics it changes
